16. DEBUGGING AND TROUBLESHOOTING
==================================

Debugging Tools:
----------------

1. Playwright Inspector:
```
npx playwright test --debug
```

Features:
- Step through tests
- Pick locators
- Edit locators live
- See actionability checks
- View console logs

Usage:
```
test('debug test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.pause(); // Pauses execution
  
  // Inspector opens automatically
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.click('#button');
});
```

2. UI Mode (Recommended):
```
npx playwright test --ui
```

Features:
- Watch mode (auto-runs on file change)
- Time travel through test execution
- Pick locators visually
- See network activity
- View console logs
- Inspect DOM

3. Trace Viewer:
```
npx playwright test --trace on
npx playwright show-trace trace.zip
```

What it captures:
- Screenshots before/after actions
- DOM snapshots
- Network requests
- Console logs
- Source code
- Action timeline

Configuration:
```
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    trace: 'on-first-retry', // or 'on', 'off', 'retain-on-failure'
  },
});
```

4. Headed Mode:
```
npx playwright test --headed
```

Watch browser as test runs (slower but visual)

5. Slow Motion:
```
npx playwright test --headed --slow-mo=1000
```

1 second delay between actions

Debugging Techniques:
---------------------

1. Console Logging:
```
test('debug with logs', async ({ page }) => {
  console.log('Starting test...');
  
  await page.goto('/');
  console.log('Current URL:', page.url());
  
  const title = await page.title();
  console.log('Page title:', title);
  
  const count = await page.locator('.item').count();
  console.log('Item count:', count);
});
```

2. Screenshots:
```
test('debug with screenshots', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.screenshot({ path: 'step1.png' });
  
  await page.click('#button');
  await page.screenshot({ path: 'step2.png' });
  
  // Element screenshot
  await page.locator('.modal').screenshot({ path: 'modal.png' });
  
  // Full page screenshot
  await page.screenshot({ path: 'full.png', fullPage: true });
});
```

3. Videos:
```
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    video: 'on', // or 'retain-on-failure', 'on-first-retry'
  },
});
```

Videos saved to test-results/

4. Browser Console:
```
test('view console logs', async ({ page }) => {
  // Listen to console
  page.on('console', msg => {
    console.log('Browser:', msg.text());
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Trigger console.log in browser
  await page.evaluate(() => console.log('Hello from browser'));
});
```

5. Network Monitoring:
```
test('monitor network', async ({ page }) => {
  // Log all requests
  page.on('request', request => {
    console.log('>>', request.method(), request.url());
  });
  
  // Log all responses
  page.on('response', response => {
    console.log('<<', response.status(), response.url());
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

Common Issues and Solutions:
-----------------------------

Issue 1: Element Not Found
```
// ❌ Problem
await page.click('#button');
// Error: Element not found

// ✓ Solution 1: Wait for element
await page.waitForSelector('#button');
await page.click('#button');

// ✓ Solution 2: Check if button exists
const buttonExists = await page.locator('#button').count() > 0;
if (buttonExists) {
  await page.click('#button');
}

// ✓ Solution 3: Use better locator
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
```

Issue 2: Element Not Clickable
```
// ❌ Problem
await page.click('#button');
// Error: Element is not visible/enabled

// ✓ Solution 1: Wait for element to be clickable
await page.waitForSelector('#button', { state: 'visible' });
await page.click('#button');

// ✓ Solution 2: Force click (use sparingly)
await page.click('#button', { force: true });

// ✓ Solution 3: Scroll into view
await page.locator('#button').scrollIntoViewIfNeeded();
await page.click('#button');
```

Issue 3: Timeout
```
// ❌ Problem
await page.click('#button');
// Error: Timeout 30000ms exceeded

// ✓ Solution 1: Increase timeout
await page.click('#button', { timeout: 60000 });

// ✓ Solution 2: Wait for page load
await page.goto('/');
await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
await page.click('#button');

// ✓ Solution 3: Wait for specific element
await page.waitForSelector('#button', { state: 'visible' });
await page.click('#button');
```

Issue 4: Flaky Tests
```
// ❌ Problem: Race condition
await page.goto('/');
await page.click('#button'); // Sometimes fails

// ✓ Solution: Use auto-waiting assertions
await page.goto('/');
await expect(page.locator('#button')).toBeVisible();
await page.click('#button');

// ✓ Solution: Wait for network
await page.goto('/');
await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
await page.click('#button');
```

Issue 5: Multiple Elements Match
```
// ❌ Problem
await page.click('.button');
// Error: strict mode violation: multiple elements match

// ✓ Solution 1: Be more specific
await page.click('.button.primary');

// ✓ Solution 2: Use first/last
await page.locator('.button').first().click();

// ✓ Solution 3: Filter by text
await page.locator('.button').filter({ hasText: 'Submit' }).click();

// ✓ Solution 4: Use getByRole
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
```

Debugging Selectors:
--------------------

1. Codegen Tool:
```
npx playwright codegen https://example.com
```

Click elements to generate selectors automatically

2. Inspector Picker:
```
await page.pause();
// Click "Pick Locator" in Inspector
// Click element in browser
// Gets optimal selector
```

3. Test Selector:
```
test('test selector', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const locator = page.locator('#my-element');
  
  // Check if exists
  const count = await locator.count();
  console.log(`Found ${count} elements`);
  
  // Get text
  if (count > 0) {
    const text = await locator.textContent();
    console.log('Text:', text);
  }
});
```

Environment-Specific Debugging:
--------------------------------

1. CI Debugging:
```
// In CI, enable trace and screenshots
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    trace: process.env.CI ? 'on-first-retry' : 'off',
    screenshot: process.env.CI ? 'only-on-failure' : 'off',
    video: process.env.CI ? 'retain-on-failure' : 'off',
  },
});
```

2. Local Debugging:
```
// Local development
npx playwright test --debug
npx playwright test --ui
npx playwright test --headed
```

3. Debug Specific Test:
```
npx playwright test login.spec.ts --debug
npx playwright test login.spec.ts:10 --debug  // Specific line
```

Advanced Debugging:
-------------------

1. Browser Context Events:
```
test('debug context', async ({ context }) => {
  context.on('page', page => {
    console.log('New page opened:', page.url());
  });
  
  context.on('request', request => {
    console.log('Request:', request.url());
  });
  
  context.on('response', response => {
    console.log('Response:', response.url(), response.status());
  });
  
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

2. HAR Files (HTTP Archive):
```
test('record HAR', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.routeFromHAR('recording.har', {
    url: '**/api/**',
    update: true, // Record new HAR
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
});

// Replay from HAR
test('replay HAR', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.routeFromHAR('recording.har');
  await page.goto('/'); // Uses recorded responses
});
```

3. Custom Test Info:
```
test('attach debugging info', async ({ page }, testInfo) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Attach screenshot
  const screenshot = await page.screenshot();
  await testInfo.attach('screenshot', {
    body: screenshot,
    contentType: 'image/png'
  });
  
  // Attach HTML
  const html = await page.content();
  await testInfo.attach('page-html', {
    body: html,
    contentType: 'text/html'
  });
  
  // Attach custom data
  await testInfo.attach('debug-info', {
    body: JSON.stringify({
      url: page.url(),
      title: await page.title(),
      timestamp: new Date().toISOString()
    }),
    contentType: 'application/json'
  });
});
```

Performance Debugging:
----------------------

1. Trace Performance:
```
test('measure performance', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const metrics = await page.evaluate(() => JSON.stringify(window.performance.timing));
  console.log('Performance:', metrics);
  
  // Navigation timing
  const timing = await page.evaluate(() => {
    const perf = window.performance.timing;
    return {
      dnsLookup: perf.domainLookupEnd - perf.domainLookupStart,
      tcpConnect: perf.connectEnd - perf.connectStart,
      responseTime: perf.responseEnd - perf.requestStart,
      domLoad: perf.domContentLoadedEventEnd - perf.navigationStart,
      pageLoad: perf.loadEventEnd - perf.navigationStart
    };
  });
  
  console.log('Timing:', timing);
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Start with UI Mode:
   ```
   npx playwright test --ui
   ```

2. ✅ Use Traces for Failed Tests:
   ```
   trace: 'on-first-retry'
   ```

3. ✅ Screenshot on Failure:
   ```
   screenshot: 'only-on-failure'
   ```

4. ✅ Use page.pause() for Step-by-Step:
   ```
   await page.pause();
   ```

5. ✅ Enable Verbose Logging:
   ```
   DEBUG=pw:api npx playwright test
   ```

6. ❌ Don't Leave Debug Code in Production:
   ```
   // Remove before committing
   await page.pause();
   test.only('debug', ...);
   ```

================================================================================

17. VISUAL REGRESSION TESTING
==============================

What is Visual Regression Testing?
-----------------------------------

Visual regression testing detects unexpected visual changes in your UI by
comparing screenshots across test runs.

Use cases:
- Detect CSS regressions
- Verify cross-browser rendering
- Catch layout breaks
- Ensure pixel-perfect designs

Basic Screenshot Comparison:
-----------------------------

First run (generates baseline):
```
test('visual test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage.png');
});
```

Creates: tests/__screenshots__/test-spec-ts/homepage-chromium.png

Subsequent runs:
- Compares against baseline
- Fails if difference detected
- Shows diff image

Full Page Screenshot:
---------------------

```
test('full page screenshot', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('full-page.png', {
    fullPage: true  // Capture entire scrollable page
  });
});
```

Element Screenshot:
-------------------

```
test('element screenshot', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Specific element
  await expect(page.locator('.hero-section')).toHaveScreenshot('hero.png');
  
  // Multiple elements
  await expect(page.locator('.header')).toHaveScreenshot('header.png');
  await expect(page.locator('.footer')).toHaveScreenshot('footer.png');
});
```

Screenshot Options:
-------------------

```
test('screenshot with options', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage.png', {
    fullPage: true,              // Capture full page
    animations: 'disabled',      // Disable CSS animations
    maxDiffPixels: 100,          // Allow 100 different pixels
    maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.2,      // Allow 20% difference
    threshold: 0.2,              // Pixel difference threshold
    mask: [page.locator('.ad')], // Hide dynamic content
    omitBackground: true,        // Transparent background
    scale: 'css',                // or 'device'
  });
});
```

Masking Dynamic Content:
-------------------------

```
test('mask dynamic elements', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage.png', {
    mask: [
      page.locator('.timestamp'),    // Hide timestamps
      page.locator('.ad-banner'),    // Hide ads
      page.locator('.random-quote'), // Hide random content
    ]
  });
});
```

Masked elements are covered with pink boxes in screenshot.

Handling Animations:
--------------------

```
test('disable animations', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('static.png', {
    animations: 'disabled'  // Pauses CSS animations and transitions
  });
});

// Or globally in config:
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    screenshot: {
      animations: 'disabled',
    }
  }
});
```

Cross-Browser Testing:
----------------------

```
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    {
      name: 'chromium',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] },
    },
    {
      name: 'firefox',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Firefox'] },
    },
    {
      name: 'webkit',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Safari'] },
    },
  ],
});
```

Generates separate baselines:
- homepage-chromium.png
- homepage-firefox.png
- homepage-webkit.png

Responsive Testing:
-------------------

```
test.describe('Responsive Design', () => {
  const viewports = [
    { width: 1920, height: 1080, name: 'desktop' },
    { width: 768, height: 1024, name: 'tablet' },
    { width: 375, height: 667, name: 'mobile' },
  ];
  
  for (const viewport of viewports) {
    test(`${viewport.name} view`, async ({ page }) => {
      await page.setViewportSize({ 
        width: viewport.width, 
        height: viewport.height 
      });
      
      await page.goto('/');
      
      await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot(`homepage-${viewport.name}.png`);
    });
  }
});
```

Updating Baselines:
-------------------

Update all screenshots:
```
npx playwright test --update-snapshots
```

Update specific test:
```
npx playwright test visual.spec.ts --update-snapshots
```

Update for specific browser:
```
npx playwright test --project=chromium --update-snapshots
```

Viewing Differences:
--------------------

Failed visual test creates:
1. homepage-actual.png (current screenshot)
2. homepage-expected.png (baseline)
3. homepage-diff.png (difference highlighted)

View in HTML report:
```
npx playwright show-report
```

Component Visual Testing:
--------------------------

```
test.describe('Button Components', () => {
  test('primary button', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/components');
    await expect(page.locator('.btn-primary')).toHaveScreenshot('btn-primary.png');
  });
  
  test('secondary button', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/components');
    await expect(page.locator('.btn-secondary')).toHaveScreenshot('btn-secondary.png');
  });
  
  test('disabled button', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/components');
    await expect(page.locator('.btn-disabled')).toHaveScreenshot('btn-disabled.png');
  });
});
```

State-Based Screenshots:
-------------------------

```
test('modal states', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Closed state
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('modal-closed.png');
  
  // Open modal
  await page.click('#open-modal');
  await expect(page.locator('.modal')).toBeVisible();
  
  // Open state
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('modal-open.png');
  
  // Hover state
  await page.hover('.modal .close-btn');
  await expect(page.locator('.modal')).toHaveScreenshot('modal-hover.png');
});
```

Dark Mode Testing:
------------------

```
test.describe('Dark Mode', () => {
  test('light theme', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/');
    await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage-light.png');
  });
  
  test('dark theme', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/');
    
    // Enable dark mode
    await page.evaluate(() => {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-theme', 'dark');
    });
    
    await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage-dark.png');
  });
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Use Meaningful Names:
   ```
   await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage-hero-section.png');
   ```

2. ✅ Mask Dynamic Content:
   ```
   mask: [page.locator('.timestamp'), page.locator('.ad')]
   ```

3. ✅ Disable Animations:
   ```
   animations: 'disabled'
   ```

4. ✅ Use Appropriate Thresholds:
   ```
   maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.1  // 10% difference allowed
   ```

5. ✅ Test Critical UI Components:
   - Login page
   - Checkout flow
   - Navigation
   - Modals/dialogs

6. ❌ Don't Screenshot Everything:
   - Focus on important visual elements
   - Avoid testing text content (use assertions instead)
   - Skip highly dynamic pages

7. ✅ Organize Screenshots:
   ```
   tests/
   ├── visual/
   │   ├── homepage.spec.ts
   │   ├── products.spec.ts
   │   └── checkout.spec.ts
   └── __screenshots__/
       └── visual/
   ```

8. ✅ Version Control Baselines:
   ```
   # Commit to git
   git add tests/__screenshots__/
   git commit -m "Update visual baselines"
   ```

CI/CD Considerations:
---------------------

1. Consistent Environment:
   - Use Docker for consistent rendering
   - Same OS, browser version, fonts
   - Dedicated screenshot update workflow

2. GitHub Actions Example:
```yaml
name: Visual Tests
on: [push]
jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
      - run: npx playwright install --with-deps
      - run: npx playwright test
      
      # Upload failures
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v3
        if: failure()
        with:
          name: screenshots-diff
          path: test-results/
```

3. Update Baselines Workflow:
```yaml
name: Update Screenshots
on: workflow_dispatch
jobs:
  update:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
      - run: npx playwright install --with-deps
      - run: npx playwright test --update-snapshots
      
      # Commit updated baselines
      - run: |
          git config user.name "GitHub Actions"
          git commit -am "Update baselines"
          git push
```

================================================================================

18. PERFORMANCE TESTING
========================

Performance Metrics:
--------------------

1. Navigation Timing:
```
test('measure page load', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const timing = await page.evaluate(() => {
    const perf = performance.timing;
    return {
      dnsLookup: perf.domainLookupEnd - perf.domainLookupStart,
      tcpConnection: perf.connectEnd - perf.connectStart,
      serverResponse: perf.responseEnd - perf.requestStart,
      domInteractive: perf.domInteractive - perf.navigationStart,
      domComplete: perf.domComplete - perf.navigationStart,
      pageLoad: perf.loadEventEnd - perf.navigationStart,
    };
  });
  
  console.log('Timing:', timing);
  
  // Assert performance budgets
  expect(timing.pageLoad).toBeLessThan(3000); // 3 seconds
  expect(timing.domInteractive).toBeLessThan(1500); // 1.5 seconds
});
```

2. Resource Timing:
```
test('resource loading', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const resources = await page.evaluate(() => {
    return performance.getEntriesByType('resource').map(r => ({
      name: r.name,
      duration: r.duration,
      size: r.transferSize,
      type: r.initiatorType
    }));
  });
  
  console.log('Resources loaded:', resources.length);
  
  // Find slow resources
  const slowResources = resources.filter(r => r.duration > 1000);
  console.log('Slow resources:', slowResources);
  
  expect(slowResources.length).toBe(0);
});
```

3. Web Vitals:
```
test('Core Web Vitals', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Largest Contentful Paint (LCP)
  const lcp = await page.evaluate(() => {
    return new Promise(resolve => {
      new PerformanceObserver(list => {
        const entries = list.getEntries();
        const lastEntry = entries[entries.length - 1];
        resolve(lastEntry.renderTime || lastEntry.loadTime);
      }).observe({ entryTypes: ['largest-contentful-paint'] });
    });
  });
  
  console.log('LCP:', lcp);
  expect(lcp).toBeLessThan(2500); // Good: < 2.5s
  
  // First Input Delay would require actual user interaction
  // Cumulative Layout Shift can be measured similarly
});
```

4. Custom Performance Marks:
```
test('measure specific operations', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await page.evaluate(() => performance.mark('search-start'));
  await page.fill('#search', 'laptop');
  await page.press('#search', 'Enter');
  await page.waitForSelector('.results');
  await page.evaluate(() => performance.mark('search-end'));
  
  const searchDuration = await page.evaluate(() => {
    performance.measure('search', 'search-start', 'search-end');
    const measure = performance.getEntriesByName('search')[0];
    return measure.duration;
  });
  
  console.log('Search took:', searchDuration, 'ms');
  expect(searchDuration).toBeLessThan(500); // 500ms budget
});
```

Network Performance:
--------------------

1. Network Request Timing:
```
test('API performance', async ({ page }) => {
  const apiTiming = [];
  
  page.on('response', response => {
    if (response.url().includes('/api/')) {
      apiTiming.push({
        url: response.url(),
        status: response.status(),
        timing: response.timing()
      });
    }
  });
  
  await page.goto('/dashboard');
  
  // Wait for all API calls
  await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
  
  console.log('API calls:', apiTiming);
  
  // Check slow APIs
  for (const api of apiTiming) {
    expect(api.timing.responseEnd).toBeLessThan(1000); // 1s max
  }
});
```

2. Payload Size:
```
test('measure page weight', async ({ page }) => {
  let totalSize = 0;
  
  page.on('response', response => {
    const size = response.headers()['content-length'];
    if (size) {
      totalSize += parseInt(size);
    }
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
  
  const totalMB = (totalSize / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2);
  console.log(`Total page size: ${totalMB} MB`);
  
  expect(totalSize).toBeLessThan(3 * 1024 * 1024); // 3MB max
});
```

3. Request Count:
```
test('count HTTP requests', async ({ page }) => {
  let requestCount = 0;
  
  page.on('request', () => requestCount++);
  
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
  
  console.log(`Total requests: ${requestCount}`);
  expect(requestCount).toBeLessThan(50); // 50 requests max
});
```

Lighthouse Integration:
------------------------

Using Playwright with Lighthouse:

```
import { test } from '@playwright/test';
import { playAudit } from 'playwright-lighthouse';
import lighthouse from 'lighthouse';

test('Lighthouse audit', async ({ page, browser }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await playAudit({
    page,
    port: 9222,
    thresholds: {
      performance: 80,
      accessibility: 90,
      'best-practices': 80,
      seo: 80,
    },
  });
});
```

Performance Budgets:
--------------------

```
// performance-budgets.ts
export const budgets = {
  pageLoad: 3000,        // 3 seconds
  domInteractive: 1500,  // 1.5 seconds
  apiResponse: 500,      // 500ms
  imageSize: 200000,     // 200KB
  jsSize: 500000,        // 500KB
  cssSize: 100000,       // 100KB
  totalSize: 3000000,    // 3MB
  requestCount: 50,      // 50 requests
};

// Use in tests:
import { budgets } from './performance-budgets';

test('performance budgets', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const metrics = await getPerformanceMetrics(page);
  
  expect(metrics.pageLoad).toBeLessThan(budgets.pageLoad);
  expect(metrics.totalSize).toBeLessThan(budgets.totalSize);
  expect(metrics.requestCount).toBeLessThan(budgets.requestCount);
});
```

Throttling (Simulate Slow Network):
------------------------------------

```
test('performance on slow network', async ({ page, context }) => {
  // Simulate slow 3G
  await context.route('**/*', async route => {
    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 500)); // 500ms delay
    await route.continue();
  });
  
  const start = Date.now();
  await page.goto('/');
  const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
  
  console.log(`Load time on slow network: ${loadTime}ms`);
  
  // Should still be usable on slow connection
  expect(loadTime).toBeLessThan(10000); // 10 seconds max
});
```

CPU Throttling:
```
test('performance on slow CPU', async ({ page }) => {
  // Emulate slow CPU (4x slowdown)
  const client = await page.context().newCDPSession(page);
  await client.send('Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate', { rate: 4 });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Measure performance with slow CPU
  const timing = await page.evaluate(() => {
    const perf = performance.timing;
    return perf.loadEventEnd - perf.navigationStart;
  });
  
  console.log(`Load time with 4x CPU slowdown: ${timing}ms`);
});
```

Memory Leaks Detection:
-----------------------

```
test('check for memory leaks', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Get initial memory
  const initialMemory = await page.evaluate(() => {
    return (performance as any).memory?.usedJSHeapSize;
  });
  
  // Perform operations that might leak
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
    await page.click('#open-modal');
    await page.click('#close-modal');
  }
  
  // Force garbage collection (Chrome only)
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    if ((window as any).gc) {
      (window as any).gc();
    }
  });
  
  // Get final memory
  const finalMemory = await page.evaluate(() => {
    return (performance as any).memory?.usedJSHeapSize;
  });
  
  const memoryGrowth = finalMemory - initialMemory;
  console.log(`Memory growth: ${(memoryGrowth / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2)} MB`);
  
  // Expect minimal memory growth
  expect(memoryGrowth).toBeLessThan(5 * 1024 * 1024); // 5MB max growth
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Set Performance Budgets:
   ```
   expect(pageLoadTime).toBeLessThan(3000);
   ```

2. ✅ Test on Different Networks:
   - Fast 3G
   - Slow 3G
   - Offline mode

3. ✅ Monitor Key Metrics:
   - LCP (Largest Contentful Paint)
   - FID (First Input Delay)
   - CLS (Cumulative Layout Shift)
   - TTI (Time to Interactive)

4. ✅ Track Performance Over Time:
   - Store metrics in database
   - Create trend charts
   - Alert on regressions

5. ✅ Test Real User Scenarios:
   - Login flow
   - Search
   - Checkout
   - Data-heavy pages

6. ❌ Don't Test in Isolation:
   - Test with realistic data volumes
   - Include third-party scripts
   - Test authenticated scenarios

This completes sections 16-18 of Part III. I'll continue with sections 19-23 next to finish Part III.

19. API TESTING
===============

Playwright's Request Context:
------------------------------

Playwright can test APIs directly without a browser, which is faster and more
efficient than UI-based API testing.

Basic API Test:
```
import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test';

test('GET request', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/users');
  
  // Status check
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
  expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
  
  // Headers
  expect(response.headers()['content-type']).toContain('application/json');
  
  // Body
  const data = await response.json();
  expect(data.users).toBeDefined();
  expect(data.users.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
});
```

HTTP Methods:
-------------

```
test.describe('CRUD Operations', () => {
  
  test('POST - Create user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.post('https://api.example.com/users', {
      data: {
        name: 'John Doe',
        email: 'john@example.com',
        role: 'user'
      }
    });
    
    expect(response.status()).toBe(201);
    
    const user = await response.json();
    expect(user.id).toBeDefined();
    expect(user.name).toBe('John Doe');
  });
  
  test('PUT - Update user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.put('https://api.example.com/users/123', {
      data: {
        name: 'Jane Doe',
        email: 'jane@example.com'
      }
    });
    
    expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
  });
  
  test('PATCH - Partial update', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.patch('https://api.example.com/users/123', {
      data: {
        name: 'Updated Name'
      }
    });
    
    expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
  });
  
  test('DELETE - Delete user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.delete('https://api.example.com/users/123');
    expect(response.status()).toBe(204);
  });
});
```

Authentication:
---------------

1. Bearer Token:
```
test('API with Bearer token', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/protected', {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': `Bearer ${process.env.API_TOKEN}`
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

2. API Key:
```
test('API with API key', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/data', {
    headers: {
      'X-API-Key': process.env.API_KEY
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

3. Basic Auth:
```
test('API with basic auth', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/secure', {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Basic ' + Buffer.from('username:password').toString('base64')
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

4. OAuth2:
```
test('API with OAuth2', async ({ request }) => {
  // Get access token
  const tokenResponse = await request.post('https://auth.example.com/token', {
    form: {
      grant_type: 'client_credentials',
      client_id: process.env.CLIENT_ID,
      client_secret: process.env.CLIENT_SECRET
    }
  });
  
  const { access_token } = await tokenResponse.json();
  
  // Use token
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/data', {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': `Bearer ${access_token}`
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

Request Options:
----------------

```
test('request with options', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.post('https://api.example.com/data', {
    // Headers
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'X-Custom-Header': 'value'
    },
    
    // JSON body
    data: {
      key: 'value'
    },
    
    // Form data
    form: {
      username: 'user',
      password: 'pass'
    },
    
    // Multipart form (file upload)
    multipart: {
      file: {
        name: 'file.pdf',
        mimeType: 'application/pdf',
        buffer: Buffer.from('file content')
      },
      field: 'value'
    },
    
    // Query parameters
    params: {
      page: 1,
      limit: 10,
      sort: 'name'
    },
    
    // Timeout
    timeout: 30000,
    
    // Fail on error status
    failOnStatusCode: true,
    
    // Follow redirects
    maxRedirects: 5
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

Response Handling:
------------------

```
test('handle response', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/users');
  
  // Status
  console.log('Status:', response.status());
  console.log('Status text:', response.statusText());
  console.log('OK:', response.ok());
  
  // Headers
  console.log('Headers:', response.headers());
  console.log('Content-Type:', response.headers()['content-type']);
  
  // URL
  console.log('URL:', response.url());
  
  // Body
  const json = await response.json();        // JSON
  const text = await response.text();        // Plain text
  const buffer = await response.body();      // Buffer
  
  // Timing (requires CDP)
  // const timing = response.timing();
});
```

API Test Patterns:
------------------

1. Setup and Teardown:
```
test.describe('User API', () => {
  let userId: string;
  
  test.beforeAll(async ({ request }) => {
    // Create test user
    const response = await request.post('/api/users', {
      data: { name: 'Test User', email: 'test@example.com' }
    });
    const user = await response.json();
    userId = user.id;
  });
  
  test('get user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.get(`/api/users/${userId}`);
    expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
  });
  
  test('update user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.patch(`/api/users/${userId}`, {
      data: { name: 'Updated Name' }
    });
    expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
  });
  
  test.afterAll(async ({ request }) => {
    // Delete test user
    await request.delete(`/api/users/${userId}`);
  });
});
```

2. Data-Driven API Tests:
```
const testCases = [
  { input: { page: 1, limit: 10 }, expectedCount: 10 },
  { input: { page: 2, limit: 5 }, expectedCount: 5 },
  { input: { page: 1, limit: 100 }, expectedCount: 100 },
];

for (const testCase of testCases) {
  test(`pagination: page=${testCase.input.page}, limit=${testCase.input.limit}`, async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.get('/api/users', {
      params: testCase.input
    });
    
    const data = await response.json();
    expect(data.users.length).toBe(testCase.expectedCount);
  });
}
```

3. Error Handling:
```
test('handle errors', async ({ request }) => {
  // 404 Not Found
  const notFound = await request.get('/api/users/nonexistent');
  expect(notFound.status()).toBe(404);
  
  // 400 Bad Request
  const badRequest = await request.post('/api/users', {
    data: { invalid: 'data' }
  });
  expect(badRequest.status()).toBe(400);
  const error = await badRequest.json();
  expect(error.message).toContain('validation failed');
  
  // 401 Unauthorized
  const unauthorized = await request.get('/api/protected');
  expect(unauthorized.status()).toBe(401);
  
  // 500 Server Error
  const serverError = await request.get('/api/error');
  expect(serverError.status()).toBe(500);
});
```

Schema Validation:
------------------

```
import Ajv from 'ajv';

const ajv = new Ajv();

const userSchema = {
  type: 'object',
  properties: {
    id: { type: 'string' },
    name: { type: 'string' },
    email: { type: 'string', format: 'email' },
    createdAt: { type: 'string', format: 'date-time' }
  },
  required: ['id', 'name', 'email']
};

test('validate response schema', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('/api/users/123');
  const user = await response.json();
  
  const validate = ajv.compile(userSchema);
  const valid = validate(user);
  
  expect(valid).toBeTruthy();
  if (!valid) {
    console.log('Validation errors:', validate.errors);
  }
});
```

Combining UI and API Tests:
----------------------------

```
test('create user via API, verify in UI', async ({ request, page }) => {
  // Create user via API (fast)
  const response = await request.post('/api/users', {
    data: {
      name: 'John Doe',
      email: 'john@example.com'
    }
  });
  
  const user = await response.json();
  
  // Verify in UI
  await page.goto(`/users/${user.id}`);
  await expect(page.locator('.user-name')).toHaveText('John Doe');
  await expect(page.locator('.user-email')).toHaveText('john@example.com');
  
  // Cleanup via API
  await request.delete(`/api/users/${user.id}`);
});
```

Custom Request Context:
-----------------------

```
import { test as base, expect } from '@playwright/test';

type MyFixtures = {
  apiContext: APIRequestContext;
};

export const test = base.extend<MyFixtures>({
  apiContext: async ({ playwright }, use) => {
    const context = await playwright.request.newContext({
      baseURL: 'https://api.example.com',
      extraHTTPHeaders: {
        'Authorization': `Bearer ${process.env.API_TOKEN}`,
        'Accept': 'application/json'
      }
    });
    
    await use(context);
    await context.dispose();
  }
});

// Usage:
test('use custom context', async ({ apiContext }) => {
  const response = await apiContext.get('/users');
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Use API Tests for Data Setup:
   ```
   // Fast: Create via API
   await request.post('/api/users', { data: userData });
   
   // Slow: Create via UI
   await page.goto('/users/new');
   await page.fill('#name', 'John');
   // ... many steps
   ```

2. ✅ Combine UI and API:
   - Setup: API
   - Test: UI
   - Teardown: API

3. ✅ Validate Status and Body:
   ```
   expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
   const data = await response.json();
   expect(data.users).toBeDefined();
   ```

4. ✅ Use Environment Variables:
   ```
   baseURL: process.env.API_URL
   ```

5. ✅ Handle Errors Gracefully:
   ```
   if (!response.ok()) {
     const error = await response.json();
     console.log('Error:', error.message);
   }
   ```

================================================================================

20. NETWORK INTERCEPTION AND MOCKING
=====================================

Why Mock Network Requests?
---------------------------

1. Speed: No real API calls → faster tests
2. Reliability: No dependency on external services
3. Edge Cases: Test error scenarios easily
4. Offline Testing: No internet required
5. Isolation: Test UI logic independently

Basic Request Interception:
----------------------------

```
test('intercept request', async ({ page }) => {
  // Intercept all requests to /api/users
  await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
    route.fulfill({
      status: 200,
      contentType: 'application/json',
      body: JSON.stringify({
        users: [
          { id: 1, name: 'John' },
          { id: 2, name: 'Jane' }
        ]
      })
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Page uses mocked data
  await expect(page.locator('.user')).toHaveCount(2);
});
```

Route Patterns:
---------------

```
// Exact URL
await page.route('https://api.example.com/users', handler);

// Glob pattern
await page.route('**/api/**', handler);
await page.route('**/*.{png,jpg,jpeg}', handler);

// Regex
await page.route(/\/api\/users\/\d+/, handler);

// Function
await page.route(url => url.pathname.startsWith('/api/'), handler);

// Multiple patterns
await page.route('**/api/**', handler1);
await page.route('**/images/**', handler2);
```

Route Actions:
--------------

1. Fulfill (Mock Response):
```
await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
  route.fulfill({
    status: 200,
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'X-Custom-Header': 'value'
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({ users: [] })
  });
});
```

2. Abort (Block Request):
```
await page.route('**/*.{png,jpg,jpeg}', route => {
  route.abort(); // Block all images
});

// With reason
await page.route('**/tracking.js', route => {
  route.abort('blockedbyclient');
});
```

3. Continue (Modify and Forward):
```
await page.route('**/api/**', route => {
  route.continue({
    headers: {
      ...route.request().headers(),
      'Authorization': 'Bearer mock-token'
    }
  });
});
```

4. Fallback (Pass to Next Handler):
```
await page.route('**/api/**', route => {
  if (route.request().url().includes('users')) {
    route.fulfill({ body: 'mocked' });
  } else {
    route.fallback(); // Let other handlers handle it
  }
});
```

Real-World Examples:
--------------------

Example 1: Mock API with Different Scenarios
```
test.describe('User List', () => {
  
  test('show users when API returns data', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
      route.fulfill({
        json: {
          users: [
            { id: 1, name: 'Alice' },
            { id: 2, name: 'Bob' }
          ]
        }
      });
    });
    
    await page.goto('/users');
    await expect(page.locator('.user')).toHaveCount(2);
  });
  
  test('show empty state when no users', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
      route.fulfill({
        json: { users: [] }
      });
    });
    
    await page.goto('/users');
    await expect(page.locator('.empty-state')).toBeVisible();
  });
  
  test('show error when API fails', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
      route.fulfill({
        status: 500,
        json: { error: 'Internal server error' }
      });
    });
    
    await page.goto('/users');
    await expect(page.locator('.error-message')).toBeVisible();
  });
  
  test('show loading state', async ({ page }) => {
    await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
      // Delay response
      setTimeout(() => {
        route.fulfill({ json: { users: [] } });
      }, 2000);
    });
    
    await page.goto('/users');
    await expect(page.locator('.loading-spinner')).toBeVisible();
  });
});
```

Example 2: Mock File Upload
```
test('mock file upload', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.route('**/api/upload', route => {
    route.fulfill({
      status: 200,
      json: {
        success: true,
        fileId: 'mock-file-123',
        url: 'https://cdn.example.com/mock-file-123.pdf'
      }
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/upload');
  await page.setInputFiles('#file-input', 'test-file.pdf');
  await page.click('#upload-button');
  
  await expect(page.locator('.success-message')).toHaveText('File uploaded successfully');
});
```

Example 3: Mock Third-Party Services
```
test('mock payment gateway', async ({ page }) => {
  // Mock Stripe
  await page.route('**/api.stripe.com/**', route => {
    route.fulfill({
      json: {
        id: 'ch_mock123',
        status: 'succeeded',
        amount: 2000
      }
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/checkout');
  await page.fill('#card-number', '4242424242424242');
  await page.click('#pay-button');
  
  await expect(page.locator('.payment-success')).toBeVisible();
});
```

HAR Files (HTTP Archive):
--------------------------

Record and replay network traffic:

1. Record HAR:
```
test('record HAR', async ({ page }) => {
  // Start recording
  await page.routeFromHAR('recording.har', {
    url: '**/api/**',
    update: true  // Record mode
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.click('#load-data');
  
  // HAR file saved with all API requests
});
```

2. Replay HAR:
```
test('replay HAR', async ({ page }) => {
  // Replay from HAR file
  await page.routeFromHAR('recording.har', {
    url: '**/api/**'
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Uses recorded responses, no actual API calls
});
```

3. Update Mode:
```
test('update HAR', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.routeFromHAR('recording.har', {
    url: '**/api/**',
    update: true,        // Update existing
    updateContent: 'embed'  // or 'attach'
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

Request Modification:
---------------------

1. Add Headers:
```
await page.route('**/api/**', route => {
  route.continue({
    headers: {
      ...route.request().headers(),
      'X-Test-Mode': 'true',
      'X-User-Id': '123'
    }
  });
});
```

2. Modify POST Data:
```
await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
  if (route.request().method() === 'POST') {
    const postData = route.request().postDataJSON();
    route.continue({
      postData: JSON.stringify({
        ...postData,
        testMode: true
      })
    });
  } else {
    route.continue();
  }
});
```

3. Redirect:
```
await page.route('**/old-api/**', route => {
  const url = route.request().url().replace('old-api', 'new-api');
  route.continue({ url });
});
```

Response Monitoring:
--------------------

```
test('monitor responses', async ({ page }) => {
  const responses = [];
  
  page.on('response', response => {
    if (response.url().includes('/api/')) {
      responses.push({
        url: response.url(),
        status: response.status(),
        timing: response.timing()
      });
    }
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  console.log('API calls made:', responses);
  
  // Assert on responses
  expect(responses.some(r => r.url.includes('/api/users'))).toBeTruthy();
  expect(responses.every(r => r.status === 200)).toBeTruthy();
});
```

Conditional Mocking:
--------------------

```
test('mock based on environment', async ({ page }) => {
  const useMocks = process.env.USE_MOCKS === 'true';
  
  if (useMocks) {
    await page.route('**/api/**', route => {
      route.fulfill({ json: mockData });
    });
  }
  
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

Performance Optimization:
--------------------------

Block unnecessary resources:

```
test('block unnecessary resources', async ({ page }) => {
  // Block images, fonts, and CSS for faster tests
  await page.route('**/*.{png,jpg,jpeg,gif,svg,woff,woff2,css}', route => {
    route.abort();
  });
  
  // Block analytics and tracking
  await page.route('**/analytics.js', route => route.abort());
  await page.route('**/tracking/**', route => route.abort());
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Page loads faster without unnecessary resources
});
```

Custom Mock Server:
-------------------

```
import { test as base } from '@playwright/test';

type MockFixtures = {
  mockAPI: void;
};

export const test = base.extend<MockFixtures>({
  mockAPI: async ({ page }, use) => {
    // Setup mocks
    await page.route('**/api/users', route => {
      route.fulfill({ json: { users: [] } });
    });
    
    await page.route('**/api/posts', route => {
      route.fulfill({ json: { posts: [] } });
    });
    
    await use();
    
    // Cleanup (if needed)
  }
});

// Usage:
test('with mocks', async ({ page, mockAPI }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  // All API calls mocked
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Mock External Services:
   ```
   await page.route('**/api.external-service.com/**', handler);
   ```

2. ✅ Use HAR for Complex Scenarios:
   ```
   await page.routeFromHAR('recording.har');
   ```

3. ✅ Test Error Cases:
   ```
   route.fulfill({ status: 500 });
   route.fulfill({ status: 404 });
   route.abort('timedout');
   ```

4. ✅ Block Unnecessary Resources:
   ```
   await page.route('**/*.{png,jpg}', route => route.abort());
   ```

5. ❌ Don't Mock Everything:
   - Mock external services
   - Keep your API calls real (or test against staging)

6. ✅ Organize Mocks:
   ```
   mocks/
   ├── users-api.ts
   ├── posts-api.ts
   └── payment-api.ts
   ```

================================================================================

21. WEBSOCKET AND REAL-TIME TESTING
====================================

WebSocket Connection:
---------------------

```
test('WebSocket connection', async ({ page }) => {
  // Listen for WebSocket
  const wsPromise = page.waitForEvent('websocket');
  
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const ws = await wsPromise;
  console.log('WebSocket URL:', ws.url());
  
  // Listen for messages
  ws.on('framesent', frame => {
    console.log('Sent:', frame.payload);
  });
  
  ws.on('framereceived', frame => {
    console.log('Received:', frame.payload);
  });
  
  ws.on('close', () => {
    console.log('WebSocket closed');
  });
});
```

Real-Time Chat Example:
------------------------

```
test('chat application', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/chat');
  
  // Wait for WebSocket connection
  const ws = await page.waitForEvent('websocket');
  
  // Wait for connection message
  const connectionFrame = await ws.waitForEvent('framereceived', {
    predicate: frame => {
      const data = JSON.parse(frame.payload as string);
      return data.type === 'connected';
    }
  });
  
  // Send message
  await page.fill('#message-input', 'Hello!');
  await page.click('#send-button');
  
  // Wait for echo message
  const echoFrame = await ws.waitForEvent('framereceived', {
    predicate: frame => {
      const data = JSON.parse(frame.payload as string);
      return data.type === 'message' && data.text === 'Hello!';
    }
  });
  
  // Verify message appears in UI
  await expect(page.locator('.message').last()).toHaveText('Hello!');
});
```

Live Updates:
-------------

```
test('live dashboard updates', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/dashboard');
  
  const ws = await page.waitForEvent('websocket');
  
  // Initial value
  await expect(page.locator('.user-count')).toHaveText('0');
  
  // Simulate server pushing update
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    const ws = new WebSocket('ws://localhost:3000');
    ws.onopen = () => {
      ws.send(JSON.stringify({
        type: 'update',
        data: { userCount: 42 }
      }));
    };
  });
  
  // Wait for update in UI
  await expect(page.locator('.user-count')).toHaveText('42');
});
```

Server-Sent Events (SSE):
--------------------------

```
test('SSE updates', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/sse-demo');
  
  // Monitor network for SSE connection
  const ssePromise = page.waitForResponse(
    response => response.url().includes('/events') && 
                response.headers()['content-type']?.includes('text/event-stream')
  );
  
  await page.click('#connect');
  
  const sse = await ssePromise;
  console.log('SSE connected');
  
  // Wait for update
  await expect(page.locator('.sse-message')).toHaveText('Event received');
});
```

Polling and Auto-Refresh:
--------------------------

```
test('auto-refresh data', async ({ page }) => {
  let requestCount = 0;
  
  await page.route('**/api/data', route => {
    requestCount++;
    route.fulfill({
      json: { value: requestCount }
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/auto-refresh');
  
  // Initial load
  await expect(page.locator('.data-value')).toHaveText('1');
  
  // Wait for auto-refresh (every 5 seconds)
  await page.waitForTimeout(6000);
  
  // Should have refreshed
  await expect(page.locator('.data-value')).toHaveText('2');
});
```

Mock WebSocket Server:
-----------------------

```
test('mock WebSocket', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Inject mock WebSocket
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    class MockWebSocket {
      onopen: any;
      onmessage: any;
      onclose: any;
      onerror: any;
      
      constructor(url: string) {
        setTimeout(() => this.onopen?.(), 100);
      }
      
      send(data: string) {
        // Echo message back
        setTimeout(() => {
          this.onmessage?.({ data });
        }, 100);
      }
      
      close() {
        setTimeout(() => this.onclose?.(), 100);
      }
    }
    
    (window as any).WebSocket = MockWebSocket;
  });
  
  // Now test with mock WebSocket
  await page.fill('#message', 'Test');
  await page.click('#send');
  
  await expect(page.locator('.message')).toHaveText('Test');
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Wait for WebSocket Connection:
   ```
   const ws = await page.waitForEvent('websocket');
   ```

2. ✅ Verify Message Content:
   ```
   ws.on('framereceived', frame => {
     const data = JSON.parse(frame.payload);
     expect(data.type).toBe('update');
   });
   ```

3. ✅ Test Connection Errors:
   ```
   ws.on('close', () => {
     expect(page.locator('.error')).toBeVisible();
   });
   ```

4. ✅ Mock for Reliability:
   - Use mock WebSocket for consistent tests
   - Real WebSocket for integration tests

================================================================================

22. DATA-DRIVEN TESTING
========================

Test Data Patterns:
-------------------

1. Inline Test Data:
```
test.describe('Login', () => {
  const testCases = [
    { username: 'user1', password: 'pass1', expected: 'success' },
    { username: 'user2', password: 'pass2', expected: 'success' },
    { username: 'invalid', password: 'wrong', expected: 'error' },
  ];
  
  for (const testCase of testCases) {
    test(`login with ${testCase.username}`, async ({ page }) => {
      await page.goto('/login');
      await page.fill('#username', testCase.username);
      await page.fill('#password', testCase.password);
      await page.click('#login');
      
      if (testCase.expected === 'success') {
        await expect(page).toHaveURL('/dashboard');
      } else {
        await expect(page.locator('.error')).toBeVisible();
      }
    });
  }
});
```

2. External JSON File:
```
// test-data/users.json
[
  { "username": "alice", "email": "alice@example.com", "role": "admin" },
  { "username": "bob", "email": "bob@example.com", "role": "user" },
  { "username": "charlie", "email": "charlie@example.com", "role": "guest" }
]

// test file
import testUsers from './test-data/users.json';

test.describe('User Registration', () => {
  for (const user of testUsers) {
    test(`register ${user.username}`, async ({ page }) => {
      await page.goto('/register');
      await page.fill('#username', user.username);
      await page.fill('#email', user.email);
      await page.selectOption('#role', user.role);
      await page.click('#submit');
      
      await expect(page.locator('.success')).toBeVisible();
    });
  }
});
```

3. CSV Files:
```
import fs from 'fs';
import { parse } from 'csv-parse/sync';

const csvContent = fs.readFileSync('test-data/users.csv', 'utf-8');
const users = parse(csvContent, { columns: true });

test.describe('CSV Data Tests', () => {
  for (const user of users) {
    test(`test ${user.username}`, async ({ page }) => {
      // Use user data
    });
  }
});
```

4. Database:
```
import { PrismaClient } from '@prisma/client';

const prisma = new PrismaClient();

test.describe('Database Tests', () => {
  test.beforeAll(async () => {
    // Seed test data
    await prisma.user.createMany({
      data: [
        { username: 'user1', email: 'user1@example.com' },
        { username: 'user2', email: 'user2@example.com' }
      ]
    });
  });
  
  test('test with DB data', async ({ page }) => {
    const users = await prisma.user.findMany();
    
    for (const user of users) {
      await page.goto(`/users/${user.id}`);
      await expect(page.locator('.username')).toHaveText(user.username);
    }
  });
  
  test.afterAll(async () => {
    await prisma.user.deleteMany();
  });
});
```

Parameterized Tests:
--------------------

```
const searchQueries = [
  { query: 'laptop', expectedResults: 50 },
  { query: 'phone', expectedResults: 30 },
  { query: 'tablet', expectedResults: 20 },
];

for (const { query, expectedResults } of searchQueries) {
  test(`search for "${query}"`, async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/');
    await page.fill('#search', query);
    await page.press('#search', 'Enter');
    
    const results = page.locator('.search-result');
    await expect(results).toHaveCount(expectedResults);
  });
}
```

Test Data Fixtures:
-------------------

```
type TestData = {
  testUser: { username: string; password: string };
};

export const test = base.extend<TestData>({
  testUser: async ({}, use) => {
    // Generate or fetch test data
    const user = {
      username: `user_${Date.now()}`,
      password: 'SecurePass123!'
    };
    
    await use(user);
    
    // Cleanup
  }
});

// Usage:
test('login', async ({ page, testUser }) => {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await page.fill('#username', testUser.username);
  await page.fill('#password', testUser.password);
  await page.click('#login');
});
```

Faker for Dynamic Data:
-----------------------

```
import { faker } from '@faker-js/faker';

test('form with random data', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/register');
  
  const userData = {
    firstName: faker.person.firstName(),
    lastName: faker.person.lastName(),
    email: faker.internet.email(),
    phone: faker.phone.number(),
    address: faker.location.streetAddress(),
    city: faker.location.city(),
    zipCode: faker.location.zipCode()
  };
  
  await page.fill('#firstName', userData.firstName);
  await page.fill('#lastName', userData.lastName);
  await page.fill('#email', userData.email);
  await page.fill('#phone', userData.phone);
  await page.fill('#address', userData.address);
  await page.fill('#city', userData.city);
  await page.fill('#zipCode', userData.zipCode);
  
  await page.click('#submit');
  
  await expect(page.locator('.success')).toBeVisible();
});
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Separate Test Data from Test Logic:
   ```
   // ✓ Good
   const testData = require('./data/users.json');
   
   // ✗ Bad
   const testData = [/* inline array */];
   ```

2. ✅ Use Descriptive Test Names:
   ```
   test(`login as ${user.role} with valid credentials`, ...)
   ```

3. ✅ Clean Up Test Data:
   ```
   test.afterEach(async () => {
     await deleteTestUser(testUser.id);
   });
   ```

4. ✅ Use Factories for Complex Data:
   ```
   const userFactory = () => ({
     username: faker.internet.userName(),
     email: faker.internet.email(),
     password: 'SecurePass123!'
   });
   ```

================================================================================

23. CI/CD INTEGRATION
=====================

GitHub Actions:
---------------

.github/workflows/playwright.yml:
```yaml
name: Playwright Tests

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  test:
    timeout-minutes: 60
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v3
    
    - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
      with:
        node-version: 18
        
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: npm ci
      
    - name: Install Playwright Browsers
      run: npx playwright install --with-deps
      
    - name: Run Playwright tests
      run: npx playwright test
      
    - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v3
      if: always()
      with:
        name: playwright-report
        path: playwright-report/
        retention-days: 30
```

Matrix Testing (Multiple Browsers/OS):
```yaml
jobs:
  test:
    strategy:
      matrix:
        os: [ubuntu-latest, windows-latest, macos-latest]
        browser: [chromium, firefox, webkit]
    
    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
    
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v3
    - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
    - run: npm ci
    - run: npx playwright install --with-deps ${{ matrix.browser }}
    - run: npx playwright test --project=${{ matrix.browser }}
```

Publish HTML Report:
```yaml
- name: Publish HTML report
  if: always()
  uses: actions/upload-pages-artifact@v2
  with:
    path: playwright-report/
    
- name: Deploy to GitHub Pages
  if: always()
  uses: actions/deploy-pages@v2
```

Environment Secrets:
```yaml
- name: Run tests
  env:
    BASE_URL: ${{ secrets.BASE_URL }}
    API_KEY: ${{ secrets.API_KEY }}
    TEST_USER: ${{ secrets.TEST_USER }}
    TEST_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.TEST_PASSWORD }}
  run: npx playwright test
```

Best Practices:
---------------

1. ✅ Use --shard for Parallel Execution:
   ```yaml
   strategy:
     matrix:
       shard: [1/4, 2/4, 3/4, 4/4]
   steps:
     - run: npx playwright test --shard=${{ matrix.shard }}
   ```

2. ✅ Cache Dependencies:
   ```yaml
   - uses: actions/cache@v3
     with:
       path: ~/.npm
       key: ${{ runner.os }}-node-${{ hashFiles('**/package-lock.json') }}
   ```

3. ✅ Run on Specific Events:
   ```yaml
   on:
     push:
     pull_request:
     schedule:
       - cron: '0 0 * * *'  # Daily at midnight
   ```

4. ✅ Set Timeouts:
   ```yaml
   timeout-minutes: 60
   ```

5. ✅ Upload Artifacts on Failure:
   ```yaml
   - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v3
     if: failure()
   ```

================================================================================
PART IV: INTERVIEW QUESTIONS BY LEVEL
================================================================================

24. BEGINNER LEVEL QUESTIONS (Q1-Q20)
======================================

Q1: What is Playwright?
------------------------
Answer:
Playwright is an open-source Node.js library developed by Microsoft for automating
web browsers (Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit) with a single unified API. It's
designed for end-to-end testing, cross-browser testing, and web automation.

Key Features:
- Auto-waiting mechanism
- Cross-browser support
- Fast and reliable
- Modern architecture
- Built-in debugging tools

Q2: Why do we need Node.js for Playwright?
-------------------------------------------
Answer:
Node.js is required because:

1. Runtime Environment: Playwright is a Node.js library that needs Node.js to execute
   JavaScript outside the browser.

2. Package Management: npm (comes with Node.js) manages Playwright installation
   and dependencies.

3. Asynchronous Operations: Node.js provides async/await support needed for
   browser automation.

4. Module System: Node.js enables importing Playwright modules and organizing code.

5. Cross-Platform: Node.js ensures same code works on Windows, macOS, and Linux.

Q3: What is npm? How is it different from npx?
-----------------------------------------------
Answer:
npm (Node Package Manager):
- Package manager for JavaScript
- Installs, manages, and updates packages
- Stores packages in node_modules/
- Commands: npm install, npm update, npm run

npx (Node Package Executor):
- Comes bundled with npm (v5.2.0+)
- Executes packages without global installation
- Runs local or temporary packages
- Commands: npx playwright test, npx playwright codegen

Key Difference:
- npm: Installs packages permanently
- npx: Executes packages (installs temporarily if needed)

Example:
```
npm install playwright          # Installs Playwright
npx playwright test            # Executes Playwright tests
```

Q4: How do you install Playwright?
-----------------------------------
Answer:
Step 1: Initialize Playwright project
```
npm init playwright@latest
```

This prompts for:
- TypeScript or JavaScript
- Test directory location
- GitHub Actions workflow
- Browser installation

Step 2: Verify installation
```
npx playwright test
```

Manual installation:
```
npm install --save-dev @playwright/test
npx playwright install
```

Q5: What browsers does Playwright support?
-------------------------------------------
Answer:
Playwright supports three major browser engines:

1. Chromium (Google Chrome, Edge)
   - Latest Chromium version
   - Desktop and mobile Chrome

2. Firefox
   - Latest Firefox version
   - Desktop and mobile Firefox

3. WebKit (Safari)
   - Latest WebKit version
   - Desktop and mobile Safari

Each browser is downloaded and managed by Playwright automatically, ensuring
consistent versions across all environments.

Q6: Explain: import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test';
--------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Breaking down the import statement:

import: ES6 keyword to load modules

{ test, expect }: Destructuring to extract specific exports
- test: Function to define test cases
- expect: Assertion library for validations

from '@playwright/test': Module location
- @playwright: Organization scope
- /test: Package name
- Resolves to node_modules/@playwright/test/

What you get:
- test(): Create and run test cases
- expect(): Make assertions with auto-retry

Q7: What is async/await in Playwright?
---------------------------------------
Answer:
async/await is JavaScript syntax for handling asynchronous operations.

async keyword:
- Marks function as asynchronous
- Enables use of await
- Always returns a Promise

await keyword:
- Pauses execution until Promise resolves
- Unwraps Promise value
- Only works inside async functions

Example:
```
test('example', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');  // Waits for navigation
  await page.click('#button');              // Waits for click
  await expect(page).toHaveURL('/next');    // Waits for URL change
});
```

Why needed:
Browser operations are asynchronous (take time). await ensures operations
complete before moving to next step.

Q8: What are fixtures in Playwright?
-------------------------------------
Answer:
Fixtures are pre-configured test dependencies that Playwright automatically
provides to tests. They handle setup and teardown automatically.

Built-in fixtures:
1. page: Fresh browser page
2. context: Browser context
3. browser: Browser instance
4. request: API client
5. browserName: Current browser name

Example:
```
test('example', async ({ page, request }) => {
  // page and request are fixtures
  // Automatically created and cleaned up
});
```

Benefits:
- No manual setup/cleanup
- Complete isolation between tests
- Reusable across tests
- Dependency injection

Q9: What is { page } in test function?
---------------------------------------
Answer:
{ page } is object destructuring that extracts the 'page' fixture from
Playwright's test context.

What Playwright does:
1. Creates new browser context
2. Creates new page in that context
3. Injects page into test via { page }
4. Test uses page
5. Automatically closes page and context after test

Without destructuring:
```
test('example', async (fixtures) => {
  const page = fixtures.page;
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

With destructuring:
```
test('example', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

Multiple fixtures:
```
test('example', async ({ page, context, request }) => {
  // Use multiple fixtures
});
```

Q10: What is the arrow function (=>) in tests?
-----------------------------------------------
Answer:
Arrow function (=>) is ES6 syntax for defining functions concisely.

Traditional function:
```
test('example', async function({ page }) {
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

Arrow function:
```
test('example', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
});
```

Benefits:
- More concise
- No 'this' binding issues
- Commonly used in modern JavaScript
- Preferred in Playwright documentation

Both work the same in Playwright tests.

Q11: How do you navigate to a URL in Playwright?
-------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Use page.goto():

```
await page.goto('https://example.com');
```

With options:
```
await page.goto('https://example.com', {
  waitUntil: 'networkidle',  // Wait until no network activity
  timeout: 30000             // 30 seconds timeout
});
```

Wait options:
- 'load': Wait for load event (default)
- 'domcontentloaded': Wait for DOMContentLoaded
- 'networkidle': Wait for network to be idle
- 'commit': Wait for navigation to commit

Navigate relative to baseURL (from config):
```
await page.goto('/products');  // Goes to baseURL + /products
```

Q12: How do you click an element?
----------------------------------
Answer:
Multiple ways:

1. By selector:
```
await page.click('#submit-button');
await page.click('.primary-btn');
await page.click('[data-testid="btn"]');
```

2. By role (recommended):
```
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
```

3. By text:
```
await page.getByText('Click me').click();
```

4. With options:
```
await page.click('#button', {
  force: true,              // Force click
  timeout: 5000,            // 5 seconds timeout
  clickCount: 2,            // Double-click
  button: 'right',          // Right-click
  modifiers: ['Control']    // Ctrl+Click
});
```

Playwright auto-waits for element to be:
- Attached to DOM
- Visible
- Stable (not animating)
- Enabled
- Ready to receive events

Q13: How do you fill an input field?
-------------------------------------
Answer:
Use page.fill() or page.type():

fill() - Recommended (clears field first):
```
await page.fill('#username', 'testuser');
await page.fill('#email', 'test@example.com');
```

By label:
```
await page.getByLabel('Email').fill('test@example.com');
```

By placeholder:
```
await page.getByPlaceholder('Enter your email').fill('test@example.com');
```

type() - Types character by character:
```
await page.type('#search', 'laptop', { delay: 100 }); // 100ms delay between keys
```

Clear field:
```
await page.fill('#input', ''); // Clear
```

Press keys:
```
await page.press('#search', 'Enter');
await page.press('#input', 'Control+A');
```

Q14: What are assertions in Playwright?
----------------------------------------
Answer:
Assertions verify expected conditions and fail tests if conditions aren't met.

Playwright's expect (auto-retrying):
```
await expect(page).toHaveURL('/dashboard');
await expect(page.locator('.message')).toHaveText('Success');
await expect(page.locator('#button')).toBeVisible();
```

Common assertions:
- toHaveURL()
- toHaveTitle()
- toHaveText()
- toContainText()
- toBeVisible()
- toBeHidden()
- toBeEnabled()
- toBeDisabled()
- toBeChecked()
- toHaveValue()
- toHaveAttribute()
- toHaveCount()

Auto-retry:
Playwright assertions retry for up to 5 seconds (default) until condition is
met or timeout occurs.

Non-auto-retrying (regular expect):
```
expect(5).toBe(5);  // Standard Jest/Jasmine expect
```

Q15: What is auto-waiting in Playwright?
-----------------------------------------
Answer:
Auto-waiting is Playwright's built-in mechanism that automatically waits for
elements to be actionable before performing actions.

Before every action, Playwright waits for element to be:
1. Attached to DOM
2. Visible
3. Stable (not animating)
4. Ready to receive events (not covered by another element)
5. Enabled (for inputs)

Example:
```
await page.click('#button');
```

Playwright automatically:
- Waits for button to exist
- Waits for button to be visible
- Waits for button to be enabled
- Waits for animations to complete
- Then clicks

No explicit waits needed!

Traditional approach (Selenium):
```
WebDriverWait wait = new WebDriverWait(driver, 10);
WebElement element = wait.until(ExpectedConditions.elementToBeClickable(By.id("button")));
element.click();
```

Playwright:
```
await page.click('#button');  // Auto-waits!
```

Q16: How do you wait for an element to be visible?
---------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Playwright handles this automatically, but explicit waits are available:

Auto-waiting (recommended):
```
await page.click('#button');  // Auto-waits for visibility
await expect(page.locator('#element')).toBeVisible();  // Auto-retries
```

Explicit wait:
```
await page.waitForSelector('#element', { state: 'visible' });
await page.waitForSelector('#element', { state: 'hidden' });
await page.waitForSelector('#element', { state: 'attached' });
```

Wait for element and use it:
```
const element = await page.waitForSelector('.modal');
await element.click();
```

With timeout:
```
await page.waitForSelector('#element', { 
  state: 'visible', 
  timeout: 10000  // 10 seconds
});
```

Best practice: Use assertions instead:
```
await expect(page.locator('#element')).toBeVisible();
```

Q17: What is test.describe()?
------------------------------
Answer:
test.describe() groups related tests together.

Basic usage:
```
test.describe('Login Feature', () => {
  test('successful login', async ({ page }) => {
    // Test 1
  });
  
  test('failed login', async ({ page }) => {
    // Test 2
  });
});
```

Benefits:
- Logical grouping
- Shared hooks (beforeEach, afterEach)
- Better test reports
- Organized test structure

With hooks:
```
test.describe('Shopping Cart', () => {
  test.beforeEach(async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/cart');
  });
  
  test('add item', async ({ page }) => {
    // Cart page already loaded
  });
  
  test('remove item', async ({ page }) => {
    // Cart page already loaded
  });
});
```

Nested groups:
```
test.describe('E-commerce', () => {
  test.describe('Products', () => {
    test('view product', async ({ page }) => {});
  });
  
  test.describe('Checkout', () => {
    test('complete purchase', async ({ page }) => {});
  });
});
```

Q18: What are beforeEach and afterEach hooks?
----------------------------------------------
Answer:
Hooks run code before/after tests for setup and cleanup.

beforeEach - Runs before each test:
```
test.describe('Tests', () => {
  test.beforeEach(async ({ page }) => {
    // Setup: Login before each test
    await page.goto('/login');
    await page.fill('#username', 'user');
    await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
    await page.click('#login');
  });
  
  test('view profile', async ({ page }) => {
    // Already logged in!
    await page.goto('/profile');
  });
  
  test('view settings', async ({ page }) => {
    // Already logged in!
    await page.goto('/settings');
  });
});
```

afterEach - Runs after each test:
```
test.afterEach(async ({ page }) => {
  // Cleanup: Clear storage
  await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.clear());
});
```

All hooks:
- beforeAll: Runs once before all tests
- beforeEach: Runs before each test
- afterEach: Runs after each test
- afterAll: Runs once after all tests

Execution order:
```
beforeAll
  beforeEach
    test 1
  afterEach
  beforeEach
    test 2
  afterEach
afterAll
```

Q19: What is the difference between Playwright and Selenium?
-------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Architecture:
- Playwright: Direct browser control via DevTools Protocol
- Selenium: HTTP-based WebDriver protocol

Auto-Waiting:
- Playwright: Built-in auto-waiting
- Selenium: Manual waits needed (WebDriverWait)

Speed:
- Playwright: Faster (direct protocol)
- Selenium: Slower (HTTP overhead)

Browser Support:
- Playwright: Chromium, Firefox, WebKit (own browser builds)
- Selenium: Any browser with WebDriver support

Modern Features:
- Playwright: Network interception, auto-waiting, trace viewer
- Selenium: Basic automation, no built-in mocking

Development:
- Playwright: Microsoft, newer (2020)
- Selenium: Open-source community, older (2004)

When to use Playwright:
- Modern web apps
- Need reliability (auto-waiting)
- Cross-browser testing
- Fast execution

When to use Selenium:
- Legacy applications
- Mature ecosystem needed
- Specific browser versions required

Q20: How do you run Playwright tests?
--------------------------------------
Answer:

Basic execution:
```bash
npx playwright test                    # Run all tests
```

**1. Headless vs Headed Mode:**
By default, tests execute in **headless mode** (browser runs without UI, faster).

```bash
# Default: Headless mode (no browser UI)
npx playwright test

# Headed mode: See browser while testing
npx playwright test --headed

# Slow motion in headed mode (1 second delay between actions)
npx playwright test --headed --slow-mo=1000
```

Configure in playwright.config.ts:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    headless: false,  // Run all tests in headed mode
  },
});
```

**2. What is a Worker?**
A worker is an independent process that runs tests in parallel. Each worker:
- Runs in isolation (separate browser instance)
- Has its own browser context
- Cannot share state with other workers
- Enables parallel test execution

```bash
# Use all CPU cores (default)
npx playwright test

# Use specific number of workers
npx playwright test --workers=4

# Run sequentially (1 worker)
npx playwright test --workers=1

# Disable parallel execution
npx playwright test --workers=1
```

Configure in playwright.config.ts:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  workers: process.env.CI ? 2 : undefined,  // 2 workers on CI, all cores locally
  // workers: 4,                             // Always use 4 workers
  // workers: 1,                             // Always sequential
});
```

Worker-scoped fixtures (shared across tests in same worker):
```typescript
export const test = base.extend<{}, { workerDatabase: Database }>({
  workerDatabase: [async ({}, use) => {
    // Setup once per worker
    const db = await setupDatabase();
    await use(db);
    await db.close();
  }, { scope: 'worker' }],
});
```

**3. Execute Specific Test:**
```bash
# Run specific file
npx playwright test tests/login.spec.ts
npx playwright test login.spec.ts

# Run multiple specific files
npx playwright test tests/login.spec.ts tests/signup.spec.ts

# Run test at specific line number
npx playwright test login.spec.ts:25

# Run tests matching a pattern (grep)
npx playwright test -g "login"
npx playwright test -g "user login"
npx playwright test --grep "login"

# Run tests NOT matching pattern
npx playwright test --grep-invert "slow"
```

**4. Run Test on Only Specific Browser:**
```bash
# Single browser
npx playwright test --project=chromium
npx playwright test --project=firefox
npx playwright test --project=webkit

# Multiple browsers
npx playwright test --project=chromium --project=firefox

# List all projects
npx playwright test --list
```

Configure specific browser in config:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    { name: 'chromium', use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] } },
    { name: 'firefox', use: { ...devices['Desktop Firefox'] } },
    { name: 'webkit', use: { ...devices['Desktop Safari'] } },
    { name: 'mobile-chrome', use: { ...devices['Pixel 5'] } },
  ],
});
```

**5. Run Only Specific Test (Spec) Files:**
```bash
# Single file
npx playwright test tests/auth/login.spec.ts

# Multiple files
npx playwright test tests/auth/login.spec.ts tests/auth/signup.spec.ts

# All files in a directory
npx playwright test tests/auth/

# Pattern matching (glob)
npx playwright test tests/auth/**/*.spec.ts
npx playwright test tests/**/*login*.spec.ts

# Exclude specific files
npx playwright test --grep-invert @slow
```

Using test.only() in code:
```typescript
test.only('run this test', async ({ page }) => {
  // Only this test will run
});

test('skip this test', async ({ page }) => {
  // This will be skipped
});
```

Configure in playwright.config.ts:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  testDir: './tests',
  testMatch: '**/*.spec.ts',           // Run files matching pattern
  testIgnore: '**/temp/**',            // Ignore these files
});
```

**6. Run Tests Based on Text (Tags/Grep):**
```bash
# Grep by test name
npx playwright test -g "login"
npx playwright test --grep "user authentication"

# Case-insensitive grep
npx playwright test -g "LOGIN" --grep-ignore-case

# Multiple patterns (OR)
npx playwright test -g "login|signup"

# Invert match (exclude)
npx playwright test --grep-invert "slow"
npx playwright test --grep-invert "@skip"

# Combine grep patterns
npx playwright test -g "login" --grep-invert "slow"
```

Using tags in test names:
```typescript
test('login @smoke @critical', async ({ page }) => {
  // Test code
});

test('profile @smoke', async ({ page }) => {
  // Test code
});

test('admin panel @slow @skip', async ({ page }) => {
  // Test code
});
```

Run tagged tests:
```bash
npx playwright test -g "@smoke"
npx playwright test -g "@critical"
npx playwright test --grep-invert "@skip"
```

**7. Run Tests in UI Mode:**
```bash
npx playwright test --ui
```

UI Mode features:
- **Time Travel**: Step through test execution
- **Watch Mode**: Auto-rerun on file changes
- **Pick Locator**: Click elements to generate selectors
- **Visual Timeline**: See screenshots at each step
- **Filter Tests**: Run specific tests
- **Debug**: Pause and inspect at any point

Best for:
- Interactive debugging
- Writing new tests
- Exploring application
- Understanding test failures

**8. Run Tests in Debug Mode:**
```bash
# Debug mode (opens Playwright Inspector)
npx playwright test --debug

# Debug specific file
npx playwright test tests/login.spec.ts --debug

# Debug specific test
npx playwright test -g "login" --debug

# Debug from specific line
npx playwright test login.spec.ts:25 --debug
```

Programmatic debugging with pause():
```typescript
test('debug test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  // Execution pauses here, opens Inspector
  await page.pause();
  
  await page.click('#button');
  await page.pause();  // Pause again
  
  await page.fill('#input', 'text');
});
```

Debug Mode features:
- **Playwright Inspector**: Step through actions
- **Pick Locator**: Generate selectors by clicking
- **Explore**: Manually interact with page
- **Record**: Generate test code from actions
- **Console**: Execute Playwright commands

Other debugging options:
```bash
# Headed + slow motion
npx playwright test --headed --slow-mo=1000

# Enable trace
npx playwright test --trace on

# Show browser console
npx playwright test --headed

# Generate test code
npx playwright codegen https://example.com
```

**Complete Command Reference:**
```bash
# Execution modes
npx playwright test                          # All tests, headless
npx playwright test --headed                 # All tests, headed
npx playwright test --ui                     # UI mode (interactive)
npx playwright test --debug                  # Debug mode

# Specific tests
npx playwright test file.spec.ts             # Specific file
npx playwright test -g "login"               # Grep by name
```
