```bash
npx playwright test file.spec.ts:25          # Specific line

# Browsers
npx playwright test --project=chromium       # Single browser
npx playwright test --project=chromium --project=firefox  # Multiple

# Workers/Parallelization
npx playwright test --workers=4              # 4 parallel workers
npx playwright test --workers=1              # Sequential

# Other options
npx playwright test --last-failed            # Only failed tests
npx playwright test --list                   # List all tests
npx playwright test --reporter=list          # Different reporter
npx playwright test --update-snapshots       # Update screenshots
npx playwright test --trace on               # Enable tracing

# Sharding (for CI)
npx playwright test --shard=1/3              # Run shard 1 of 3
```

View results:
```bash
npx playwright show-report                   # HTML report
npx playwright show-trace trace.zip          # Trace viewer
```

================================================================================

25. INTERMEDIATE LEVEL QUESTIONS (Q21-Q40)
===========================================

Q21: What is a locator in Playwright?
--------------------------------------
Answer:
A locator is Playwright's way of finding elements on a page. Unlike traditional
selectors, locators are lazy, strict, and auto-retrying.

Key characteristics:
1. Lazy: Don't search until action is performed
2. Strict: Fail if multiple elements match
3. Auto-retrying: Automatically retry actions until timeout

Creating locators:
```
const button = page.locator('#submit');  // CSS selector
const heading = page.getByRole('heading', { level: 1 });  // By role
const input = page.getByLabel('Email');  // By label
const link = page.getByText('Click here');  // By text
```

Traditional approach (finds once):
```
const element = await page.$('#button');  // Can become stale
```

Playwright approach (finds fresh each time):
```
const button = page.locator('#button');
await button.click();  // Finds fresh, then clicks
await button.fill('text');  // Finds fresh, then fills
```

Benefits:
- No stale element errors
- Automatic retry
- Works with dynamic content

Q22: What is the difference between page.locator() and page.$?
---------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

page.locator() - Recommended:
```
const button = page.locator('#submit');
await button.click();  // Auto-waits, auto-retries
```

Features:
- Lazy evaluation (doesn't search immediately)
- Auto-waiting
- Auto-retrying
- Strict mode (fails if multiple matches)
- Chainable

page.$() - Discouraged:
```
const button = await page.$('#submit');
await button?.click();
```

Issues:
- Evaluates immediately
- No auto-waiting
- No auto-retrying
- Can become stale
- Returns null if not found

Comparison:
```
// ❌ Old way (can fail randomly)
const element = await page.$('#button');
if (element) {
  await element.click();
}

// ✓ New way (reliable)
await page.locator('#button').click();
```

Use locators for all modern Playwright tests!

Q23: Explain different ways to select elements (getByRole, getByText, etc.)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

1. getByRole (Best for accessibility):
```
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
await page.getByRole('textbox', { name: 'Email' }).fill('test@example.com');
await page.getByRole('heading', { level: 1 }).textContent();
```

Available roles: button, link, textbox, checkbox, radio, heading, listitem, etc.

2. getByText (User-visible text):
```
await page.getByText('Click me').click();
await page.getByText(/click me/i).click();  // Regex, case-insensitive
await page.getByText('Click', { exact: true }).click();  // Exact match
```

3. getByLabel (Form inputs):
```
await page.getByLabel('Email address').fill('test@example.com');
await page.getByLabel('Remember me').check();
```

4. getByPlaceholder:
```
await page.getByPlaceholder('Enter your email').fill('test@example.com');
```

5. getByTestId (Best for testing):
```
await page.getByTestId('submit-btn').click();
```

Requires: data-testid attribute
```html
<button data-testid="submit-btn">Submit</button>
```

6. getByAltText (Images):
```
await page.getByAltText('User avatar').click();
```

7. getByTitle:
```
await page.getByTitle('Close').click();
```

8. CSS Selectors (page.locator):
```
await page.locator('#id').click();
await page.locator('.class').click();
await page.locator('[data-attr="value"]').click();
```

Priority (Most to Least recommended):
1. getByRole (accessible)
2. getByTestId (testing)
3. getByLabel/getByPlaceholder (user-facing)
4. getByText (careful with text changes)
5. CSS selectors (brittle)

Q24: What is Page Object Model (POM) in Playwright?
----------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Page Object Model is a design pattern that creates object repository for web
elements and actions, improving test maintainability.

Without POM (bad):
```
test('login', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await page.fill('#username', 'user');
  await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
  await page.click('#login-btn');
});
```

Problems:
- Duplicate code if login needed in multiple tests
- Hard to maintain (if #username changes, update everywhere)

With POM (good):
```
// LoginPage.ts
export class LoginPage {
  constructor(private page: Page) {}
  
  async goto() {
    await this.page.goto('/login');
  }
  
  async login(username: string, password: string) {
    await this.page.fill('#username', username);
    await this.page.fill('#password', password);
    await this.page.click('#login-btn');
  }
}

// Test file
import { LoginPage } from './pages/LoginPage';

test('login', async ({ page }) => {
  const loginPage = new LoginPage(page);
  await loginPage.goto();
  await loginPage.login('user', 'pass');
});
```

Benefits:
- Single source of truth for selectors
- Reusable across tests
- Easy to maintain
- Readable tests
- Encapsulation

Q25: How do you handle multiple elements?
------------------------------------------
Answer:

Count elements:
```
const items = page.locator('.item');
const count = await items.count();
console.log(`Found ${count} items`);
```

Get all elements:
```
const items = await page.locator('.item').all();

for (const item of items) {
  const text = await item.textContent();
  console.log(text);
}
```

First/Last element:
```
await page.locator('.item').first().click();
await page.locator('.item').last().click();
```

Nth element:
```
await page.locator('.item').nth(2).click();  // Zero-indexed (3rd item)
```

Filter elements:
```
const items = page.locator('.item');
const activeItems = items.filter({ hasText: 'Active' });
const featuredItems = items.filter({ has: page.locator('.featured-badge') });
```

Iterate with index:
```
const items = page.locator('.item');
const count = await items.count();

for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
  const item = items.nth(i);
  await item.click();
}
```

Assert count:
```
await expect(page.locator('.item')).toHaveCount(10);
```

Q26: What is storage state in Playwright?
------------------------------------------
Answer:
Storage state captures and restores browser context state (cookies, localStorage,
sessionStorage) to avoid repeated login in tests.

Problem:
```
test('test 1', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await page.fill('#username', 'user');
  await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
  await page.click('#login');  // 2-3 seconds
  
  // Actual test
});

test('test 2', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await page.fill('#username', 'user');
  await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
  await page.click('#login');  // Another 2-3 seconds!
  
  // Actual test
});
```

Solution with storage state:

1. Global setup (auth.setup.ts):
```
import { test as setup } from '@playwright/test';

setup('authenticate', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/login');
  await page.fill('#username', 'user');
  await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
  await page.click('#login');
  await page.waitForURL('/dashboard');
  
  // Save storage state
  await page.context().storageState({ path: 'auth.json' });
});
```

2. Configure Playwright (playwright.config.ts):
```
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    storageState: 'auth.json',  // All tests use this state
  },
});
```

3. Tests now start logged in:
```
test('test 1', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/profile');  // Already logged in!
});

test('test 2', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/settings');  // Already logged in!
});
```

Benefits:
- Login once, use everywhere
- Faster test execution
- No repeated login code
- Works with session cookies, JWT tokens, localStorage

Q27: How do you handle iframes in Playwright?
----------------------------------------------
Answer:

Use frameLocator():

```
// Locate iframe
const frame = page.frameLocator('iframe#myframe');

// Elements inside iframe
await frame.locator('#username').fill('user');
await frame.locator('#password').fill('pass');
await frame.getByRole('button', { name: 'Login' }).click();
```

Nested iframes:
```
const outerFrame = page.frameLocator('iframe#outer');
const innerFrame = outerFrame.frameLocator('iframe#inner');
await innerFrame.locator('#button').click();
```

With name attribute:
```
const frame = page.frameLocator('iframe[name="payment"]');
await frame.locator('#card-number').fill('4242424242424242');
```

Old approach (discouraged):
```
const frame = await page.frame({ name: 'myframe' });
await frame.click('#button');
```

Key points:
- frameLocator() is recommended (returns FrameLocator)
- Auto-waits for iframe to load
- No need to switch context manually
- Works with cross-origin iframes

Q28: What is test.use() and when would you use it?
---------------------------------------------------
Answer:
test.use() applies configuration or fixtures to specific tests or test groups.

Set viewport size:
```
test.use({ viewport: { width: 1920, height: 1080 } });

test('desktop test', async ({ page }) => {
  // Runs with 1920x1080 viewport
});
```

Set browser context options:
```
test.use({ 
  locale: 'de-DE',
  timezoneId: 'Europe/Berlin',
  permissions: ['geolocation'],
  geolocation: { latitude: 52.52, longitude: 13.41 },
});
```

Use different storage state:
```
test.describe('Admin tests', () => {
  test.use({ storageState: 'admin-auth.json' });
  
  test('admin panel', async ({ page }) => {
    // Logged in as admin
  });
});

test.describe('User tests', () => {
  test.use({ storageState: 'user-auth.json' });
  
  test('user dashboard', async ({ page }) => {
    // Logged in as regular user
  });
});
```

Mobile emulation:
```
import { devices } from '@playwright/test';

test.use(devices['iPhone 12']);

test('mobile test', async ({ page }) => {
  // Runs as iPhone 12
});
```

Use cases:
- Different user roles (admin, user, guest)
- Different locales/timezones
- Different viewport sizes
- Different browser contexts
- Feature flags

Q29: How do you handle alerts, confirms, and prompts?
------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Automatically accept/dismiss:

Accept alert/confirm:
```
page.on('dialog', dialog => {
  console.log(dialog.message());
  dialog.accept();
});

await page.click('#show-alert');
```

Dismiss confirm:
```
page.on('dialog', dialog => {
  dialog.dismiss();
});

await page.click('#show-confirm');
```

Prompt with input:
```
page.on('dialog', dialog => {
  dialog.accept('My input text');
});

await page.click('#show-prompt');
```

With assertions:
```
page.on('dialog', async dialog => {
  expect(dialog.type()).toBe('alert');
  expect(dialog.message()).toBe('Are you sure?');
  await dialog.accept();
});
```

Wait for dialog:
```
const dialogPromise = page.waitForEvent('dialog');
await page.click('#show-dialog');
const dialog = await dialogPromise;
await dialog.accept();
```

Dialog types:
- 'alert': window.alert()
- 'confirm': window.confirm()
- 'prompt': window.prompt()
- 'beforeunload': Page unload confirmation

Important: Must set up listener BEFORE triggering dialog!

Q30: What is the difference between page.fill() and page.type()?
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

page.fill() - Recommended:
```
await page.fill('#username', 'testuser');
```

Behavior:
- Clears input field first
- Sets value instantly
- Faster
- Triggers 'input' and 'change' events
- Best for form filling

page.type() - Character-by-character:
```
await page.type('#search', 'laptop', { delay: 100 });
```

Behavior:
- Types each character separately
- Slower
- Can add delay between keystrokes
- Triggers keydown, keypress, keyup events
- Simulates real typing
- Best for testing autocomplete, search suggestions

When to use each:

Use fill():
- Standard form inputs
- Faster test execution
- Most use cases

Use type():
- Testing autocomplete
- Search suggestions
- Character-by-character validation
- Simulating slow typing
- Testing input masks

Example comparison:
```
// Fast: Sets value instantly
await page.fill('#email', 'test@example.com');

// Slow: Types each character
await page.type('#search', 'laptop', { delay: 50 });
```

Q31: How do you take screenshots in Playwright?
------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Full page screenshot:
```
await page.screenshot({ path: 'screenshot.png' });
await page.screenshot({ path: 'full.png', fullPage: true });
```

Element screenshot:
```
await page.locator('.hero').screenshot({ path: 'hero.png' });
```

Screenshot to buffer (no file):
```
const buffer = await page.screenshot();
```

Options:
```
await page.screenshot({
  path: 'screenshot.png',
  fullPage: true,              // Capture entire scrollable page
  type: 'png',                 // or 'jpeg'
  quality: 80,                 // JPEG quality (0-100)
  omitBackground: true,        // Transparent background
  clip: {                      // Capture specific area
    x: 0,
    y: 0,
    width: 800,
    height: 600
  },
  timeout: 30000,              // 30 seconds
  scale: 'device',             // or 'css'
});
```

Screenshot on failure (config):
```
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    screenshot: 'only-on-failure',  // or 'on', 'off'
  },
});
```

In test:
```
test('example', async ({ page }, testInfo) => {
  // Test code
  
  if (testInfo.status !== 'passed') {
    await page.screenshot({ path: `failure-${Date.now()}.png` });
  }
});
```

Visual regression testing:
```
await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage.png');
```

Q32: What is networkidle in Playwright?
----------------------------------------
Answer:
'networkidle' is a page load state that indicates no network activity for at
least 500ms.

Usage:
```
await page.goto('https://example.com', {
  waitUntil: 'networkidle'
});
```

Wait states:
1. 'load': Wait for load event (default)
2. 'domcontentloaded': Wait for DOMContentLoaded event
3. 'networkidle': Wait for 500ms of no network activity
4. 'commit': Wait for navigation to commit

When to use networkidle:
- Page makes AJAX requests after initial load
- Dynamic content loading
- SPAs (Single Page Applications)
- Ensuring all API calls complete

Example:
```
// Wait for all API calls to finish
await page.goto('/dashboard', { waitUntil: 'networkidle' });
```

Or separately:
```
await page.goto('/dashboard');
await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
```

Caution:
- Can be slow if page has polling/websockets
- Use sparingly
- Prefer waiting for specific elements instead:
```
await page.goto('/dashboard');
await page.waitForSelector('.data-loaded');
```

Q33: How do you handle file uploads?
-------------------------------------
Answer:

Single file upload:
```
await page.setInputFiles('#file-input', 'path/to/file.pdf');
```

Multiple files:
```
await page.setInputFiles('#file-input', [
  'file1.pdf',
  'file2.pdf',
  'file3.pdf'
]);
```

Using getByLabel:
```
await page.getByLabel('Upload file').setInputFiles('file.pdf');
```

From buffer:
```
await page.setInputFiles('#file-input', {
  name: 'file.pdf',
  mimeType: 'application/pdf',
  buffer: Buffer.from('file content')
});
```

Clear file input:
```
await page.setInputFiles('#file-input', []);
```

With dialog:
```
const fileChooserPromise = page.waitForEvent('filechooser');
await page.click('#upload-button');
const fileChooser = await fileChooserPromise;
await fileChooser.setFiles('file.pdf');
```

Multiple files with dialog:
```
const fileChooserPromise = page.waitForEvent('filechooser');
await page.click('#upload-button');
const fileChooser = await fileChooserPromise;
await fileChooser.setFiles(['file1.pdf', 'file2.pdf']);
```

Verify upload:
```
await page.setInputFiles('#file-input', 'file.pdf');
await page.click('#upload-btn');

await expect(page.locator('.upload-success')).toBeVisible();
await expect(page.locator('.filename')).toHaveText('file.pdf');
```

Q34: What are test annotations in Playwright?
----------------------------------------------
Answer:
Test annotations add metadata and control test execution.

test.skip() - Skip test:
```
test.skip('not ready yet', async ({ page }) => {
  // This won't run
});

// Conditional skip
test('browser-specific', async ({ page, browserName }) => {
  test.skip(browserName === 'webkit', 'Safari not supported');
});
```

test.only() - Run only this test:
```
test.only('debug this', async ({ page }) => {
  // Only this test runs
});
```

test.fixme() - Known broken test:
```
test.fixme('has bug #123', async ({ page }) => {
  // Marked as broken, skipped in reports
});
```

test.slow() - Triple timeout:
```
test('slow operation', async ({ page }) => {
  test.slow();  // Gets 3x timeout
  
  await page.goto('/generate-report');
});
```

test.fail() - Expect failure:
```
test('known browser bug', async ({ page }) => {
  test.fail();  // Test failing = pass, test passing = fail
});
```

test.setTimeout():
```
test('custom timeout', async ({ page }) => {
  test.setTimeout(120000);  // 2 minutes for this test
});
```

Annotations with metadata:
```
test('annotated test', {
  annotation: { type: 'issue', description: 'Bug #123' }
}, async ({ page }) => {
  // Test code
});
```

Tags (Playwright 1.42+):
```
test('login @smoke @critical', async ({ page }) => {
  // Tagged test
});

// Run:
// npx playwright test --grep @smoke
```

Q35: How do you debug Playwright tests?
----------------------------------------
Answer:

**1. UI Mode (Best for Interactive Debugging):**
```bash
npx playwright test --ui
```

Features:
- ✅ **Time Travel**: Step backward/forward through test execution
- ✅ **Watch Mode**: Auto-rerun tests on file changes
- ✅ **Pick Locator**: Click elements to generate selectors
- ✅ **Visual Timeline**: See screenshots at each action
- ✅ **Filter Tests**: Search and run specific tests
- ✅ **Pause & Resume**: Stop at any point to inspect
- ✅ **Source Code**: View test code with highlighted current line

When to use:
- Writing new tests
- Understanding test failures
- Exploring the application
- Finding the right selectors

**2. Debug Mode (Playwright Inspector):**
```bash
# Debug all tests
npx playwright test --debug

# Debug specific file
npx playwright test tests/login.spec.ts --debug

# Debug specific test by name
npx playwright test -g "user login" --debug

# Debug from specific line
npx playwright test login.spec.ts:25 --debug
```

Features:
- Step through each action
- Pick locator tool
- See action log
- Explore page
- Record actions
- Console access

Programmatic pause:
```typescript
test('debug test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  // Execution pauses here - opens Inspector
  await page.pause();
  
  await page.click('#button');
  
  // Pause again at any point
  await page.pause();
  
  await page.fill('#input', 'text');
});
```

**3. Headed Mode (See Browser While Testing):**
```bash
# Run with visible browser
npx playwright test --headed

# Run specific browser headed
npx playwright test --project=chromium --headed

# Headed mode with slow motion (1 second delay)
npx playwright test --headed --slow-mo=1000

# Slow motion with specific delay (500ms)
npx playwright test --headed --slow-mo=500
```

Configure in playwright.config.ts:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    headless: false,  // All tests run headed
    slowMo: 1000,     // 1 second delay between actions
  },
});
```

**4. Headless Mode (Default - Faster):**
```bash
# Default: Headless
npx playwright test

# Explicitly headless
npx playwright test --headed=false
```

Headless vs Headed comparison:
```typescript
// Headless (default)
// - No browser UI
// - Faster execution
// - Less resource usage
// - Best for CI/CD
// - Can't watch test execution

// Headed
// - Shows browser UI
// - Slower execution
// - More resource usage
// - Best for debugging
// - Watch test execution
```

**5. Trace Viewer (Post-Mortem Debugging):**
```bash
# Enable tracing
npx playwright test --trace on

# Trace on first retry only (recommended)
npx playwright test --trace on-first-retry

# Trace only on failure
npx playwright test --trace retain-on-failure

# View trace file
npx playwright show-trace trace.zip
npx playwright show-trace test-results/example-test/trace.zip
```

Configure in playwright.config.ts:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    trace: 'on-first-retry',        // Trace on retry
    // trace: 'on',                 // Always trace (slow)
    // trace: 'off',                // Never trace
    // trace: 'retain-on-failure', // Only failed tests
  },
});
```

Trace includes:
- 📸 Screenshots at every action
- 🌳 DOM snapshots (can inspect HTML)
- 🌐 Network activity (all requests/responses)
- 📝 Console logs
- 📋 Source code
- ⏱️ Timeline of events

**6. Codegen (Generate Test Code):**
```bash
# Generate test from scratch
npx playwright codegen

# Start from URL
npx playwright codegen https://example.com

# Generate with specific browser
npx playwright codegen --browser=firefox https://example.com

# Generate with device emulation
npx playwright codegen --device="iPhone 13" https://example.com

# Generate with viewport size
npx playwright codegen --viewport-size=1280,720 https://example.com

# Save to file
npx playwright codegen --target=javascript -o tests/generated.spec.js https://example.com
```

Features:
- Record actions as you interact
- Generates Playwright code
- Pick locator tool
- Copy selector
- Assert visibility/text/value

**7. Console Logging:**
```typescript
test('debug with logs', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  // Log current URL
  console.log('Current URL:', page.url());
  
  // Log element count
  const items = await page.locator('.item').count();
  console.log('Items found:', items);
  
  // Log element text
  const text = await page.locator('h1').textContent();
  console.log('Heading:', text);
  
  // Log page title
  console.log('Title:', await page.title());
});
```

**8. Screenshots for Debugging:**
```typescript
test('debug with screenshots', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Full page screenshot
  await page.screenshot({ path: 'debug-full.png', fullPage: true });
  
  // Viewport screenshot
  await page.screenshot({ path: 'debug-viewport.png' });
  
  // Element screenshot
  await page.locator('.problem-area').screenshot({ path: 'debug-element.png' });
  
  // Screenshot with timestamp
  const timestamp = Date.now();
  await page.screenshot({ path: `debug-${timestamp}.png` });
});
```

Auto-screenshot on failure:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    screenshot: 'only-on-failure',  // or 'on', 'off'
    video: 'retain-on-failure',     // Record video
  },
});
```

**9. Browser Console Monitoring:**
```typescript
test('monitor console', async ({ page }) => {
  const logs: string[] = [];
  const errors: string[] = [];
  
  // Listen to browser console
  page.on('console', msg => {
    console.log(`Browser ${msg.type()}: ${msg.text()}`);
    logs.push(msg.text());
  });
  
  // Listen to page errors
  page.on('pageerror', error => {
    console.log(`Page error: ${error.message}`);
    errors.push(error.message);
  });
  
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.click('#button');
  
  // Assert no errors
  expect(errors.length).toBe(0);
});
```

**10. Network Activity Debugging:**
```typescript
test('debug network', async ({ page }) => {
  const requests: any[] = [];
  const responses: any[] = [];
  
  page.on('request', request => {
    console.log('→', request.method(), request.url());
    requests.push({
      url: request.url(),
      method: request.method(),
      headers: request.headers()
    });
  });
  
  page.on('response', response => {
    console.log('←', response.status(), response.url());
    responses.push({
      url: response.url(),
      status: response.status()
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/dashboard');
  
  // Check for failed requests
  const failed = responses.filter(r => r.status >= 400);
  console.log('Failed requests:', failed);
});
```

**11. Test Info for Debugging:**
```typescript
test('debug with test info', async ({ page }, testInfo) => {
  console.log('Test name:', testInfo.title);
  console.log('File:', testInfo.file);
  console.log('Line:', testInfo.line);
  console.log('Column:', testInfo.column);
  console.log('Retry:', testInfo.retry);
  console.log('Worker:', testInfo.workerIndex);
  console.log('Project:', testInfo.project.name);
  
  // Save artifacts with test info
  const screenshot = testInfo.outputPath('screenshot.png');
  await page.screenshot({ path: screenshot });
  
  // Attach to report
  await testInfo.attach('screenshot', { path: screenshot });
});
```

**12. Conditional Breakpoints:**
```typescript
test('conditional pause', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/products');
  
  const items = await page.locator('.product').all();
  
  for (let i = 0; i < items.length; i++) {
    const price = await items[i].locator('.price').textContent();
    const priceNum = parseFloat(price!.replace(/[^0-9.]/g, ''));
    
    // Pause only if price is unexpected
    if (priceNum > 1000) {
      console.log(`⚠️ High price found: $${priceNum}`);
      await page.pause();  // Opens Inspector
    }
  }
});
```

**13. Slow Motion (Visual Debugging):**
```typescript
// In test
test.use({ 
  launchOptions: { 
    slowMo: 1000  // 1 second delay between actions
  } 
});

test('slow motion test', async ({ page }) => {
  // All actions delayed by 1 second
  await page.goto('/');
  await page.click('#button');
});
```

**14. Step-by-Step with Test Steps:**
```typescript
test('step by step', async ({ page }) => {
  await test.step('Navigate to login', async () => {
    await page.goto('/login');
    console.log('✓ Navigated');
  });
  
  await test.step('Enter credentials', async () => {
    await page.fill('#username', 'user');
    await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
    console.log('✓ Credentials entered');
  });
  
  await test.step('Submit form', async () => {
    await page.click('#login');
    console.log('✓ Form submitted');
  });
  
  await test.step('Verify logged in', async () => {
    await expect(page.locator('.welcome')).toBeVisible();
    console.log('✓ Logged in successfully');
  });
});
```

**Complete Debugging Workflow:**
```bash
# 1. First run: Identify failure
npx playwright test

# 2. Run in UI mode to understand failure
npx playwright test --ui

# 3. Run specific test in debug mode
npx playwright test -g "failing test" --debug

# 4. If still unclear, enable trace
npx playwright test --trace on

# 5. View trace after failure
npx playwright show-trace test-results/.../trace.zip

# 6. Fix and run headed to verify
npx playwright test --headed

# 7. Final verification
npx playwright test
```

**Debugging Command Reference:**
```bash
# Interactive modes
npx playwright test --ui                     # UI mode (best)
npx playwright test --debug                  # Debug mode
npx playwright test --headed                 # See browser
npx playwright test --headed --slow-mo=1000  # Slow motion

# Generate tests
npx playwright codegen https://example.com   # Record actions

# Traces
npx playwright test --trace on               # Enable trace
npx playwright show-trace trace.zip          # View trace

# Specific test debugging
npx playwright test file.spec.ts --debug     # Debug file
npx playwright test -g "login" --debug       # Debug by name
npx playwright test file.spec.ts:25 --debug  # Debug line

# Other
npx playwright test --reporter=list          # Detailed output
npx playwright test --workers=1 --headed     # Sequential + visible
```

Q36: What is the Page Object Model fixture pattern?
----------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Create a fixture that provides all page objects to tests.

Setup:

1. Page Objects:
```
// pages/LoginPage.ts
export class LoginPage {
  constructor(private page: Page) {}
  
  async login(username: string, password: string) {
    await this.page.fill('#username', username);
    await this.page.fill('#password', password);
    await this.page.click('#login');
  }
}

// pages/DashboardPage.ts
export class DashboardPage {
  constructor(private page: Page) {}
  
  async expectWelcome(name: string) {
    await expect(this.page.locator('.welcome')).toHaveText(`Welcome, ${name}`);
  }
}
```

2. Page Factory:
```
// pages/index.ts
export class Pages {
  readonly loginPage: LoginPage;
  readonly dashboardPage: DashboardPage;
  
  constructor(page: Page) {
    this.loginPage = new LoginPage(page);
    this.dashboardPage = new DashboardPage(page);
  }
}
```

3. Custom Fixture:
```
// fixtures.ts
import { test as base } from '@playwright/test';
import { Pages } from './pages';

type MyFixtures = {
  pages: Pages;
};

export const test = base.extend<MyFixtures>({
  pages: async ({ page }, use) => {
    await use(new Pages(page));
  }
});

export { expect } from '@playwright/test';
```

4. Use in Tests:
```
import { test, expect } from './fixtures';

test('login flow', async ({ pages }) => {
  await pages.loginPage.login('user', 'pass');
  await pages.dashboardPage.expectWelcome('user');
});
```

Benefits:
- Clean test code
- Reusable page objects
- Type-safe
- Single import
- Easy to maintain

Q37: How do you handle dropdowns/select elements?
--------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Select by value:
```
await page.selectOption('#country', 'USA');
```

Select by label:
```
await page.selectOption('#country', { label: 'United States' });
```

Select by index:
```
await page.selectOption('#country', { index: 2 });
```

Multiple select:
```
await page.selectOption('#countries', ['USA', 'Canada', 'Mexico']);
```

Using locator:
```
await page.locator('#country').selectOption('USA');
```

Get selected option:
```
const value = await page.locator('#country').inputValue();
console.log('Selected:', value);
```

Custom dropdowns (not <select>):
```
// Click to open
await page.click('#custom-dropdown');

// Select option
await page.click('text=Option 1');

// Or by role
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Select' }).click();
await page.getByRole('option', { name: 'Option 1' }).click();
```

Verify selected:
```
await expect(page.locator('#country')).toHaveValue('USA');
```

Get all options:
```
const options = await page.locator('#country option').allTextContents();
console.log('Available options:', options);
```

Q38: What is test.step() and why use it?
-----------------------------------------
Answer:
test.step() breaks tests into logical steps for better reports and debugging.

Basic usage:
```
test('checkout flow', async ({ page }) => {
  await test.step('Add to cart', async () => {
    await page.goto('/products/123');
    await page.click('#add-to-cart');
  });
  
  await test.step('Proceed to checkout', async () => {
    await page.goto('/cart');
    await page.click('#checkout');
  });
  
  await test.step('Fill shipping info', async () => {
    await page.fill('#address', '123 Main St');
    await page.fill('#city', 'Boston');
  });
  
  await test.step('Complete payment', async () => {
    await page.fill('#card', '4111111111111111');
    await page.click('#submit');
  });
});
```

Benefits:
1. Better test reports (shows which step failed)
2. Easier debugging
3. Clear test structure
4. Screenshots per step

Failed output:
```
✓ Add to cart
✓ Proceed to checkout
✗ Fill shipping info
  - Error: Element not found #address
✕ Complete payment (skipped)
```

With return values:
```
const userId = await test.step('Create user', async () => {
  const response = await request.post('/api/users', {
    data: { name: 'Test User' }
  });
  return (await response.json()).id;
});

await test.step('Verify user', async () => {
  await page.goto(`/users/${userId}`);
  await expect(page.locator('.name')).toHaveText('Test User');
});
```

Nested steps:
```
await test.step('Checkout', async () => {
  await test.step('Enter shipping', async () => {
    // ...
  });
  
  await test.step('Enter payment', async () => {
    // ...
  });
});
```

Q39: How do you test file downloads?
-------------------------------------
Answer:

Wait for download:
```
test('download file', async ({ page }) => {
  const downloadPromise = page.waitForEvent('download');
  await page.click('#download-button');
  const download = await downloadPromise;
  
  // Get file path
  const path = await download.path();
  console.log('Downloaded to:', path);
  
  // Save to specific location
  await download.saveAs('/path/to/save/file.pdf');
  
  // Get file name
  console.log('Filename:', download.suggestedFilename());
});
```

Verify download:
```
const download = await downloadPromise;
expect(download.suggestedFilename()).toBe('report.pdf');

// Verify file content
const path = await download.path();
const fs = require('fs');
const content = fs.readFileSync(path);
expect(content.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
```

Multiple downloads:
```
const [download1, download2] = await Promise.all([
  page.waitForEvent('download'),
  page.waitForEvent('download'),
  page.click('#download-all')
]);
```

Cancel download:
```
const download = await downloadPromise;
await download.cancel();
```

Download from link:
```
const downloadPromise = page.waitForEvent('download');
await page.click('a[href="/files/report.pdf"]');
const download = await downloadPromise;
```

Q40: How do you run tests in parallel?
---------------------------------------
Answer:

Playwright runs tests in parallel by default using **workers**!

**Understanding Workers:**
A worker is an independent process that:
- Runs tests in complete isolation
- Has its own browser instance
- Cannot share state with other workers
- Enables true parallel execution
- Speeds up test suite significantly

Configuration (playwright.config.ts):
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  fullyParallel: true,  // Run tests in parallel (default)
  workers: 4,            // 4 parallel workers
});
```

**Worker Configuration Options:**
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  // Use all CPU cores (default)
  workers: undefined,
  
  // Use specific number
  workers: 4,
  
  // Sequential execution
  workers: 1,
  
  // Different for CI vs local
  workers: process.env.CI ? 2 : undefined,
  
  // Percentage of cores
  workers: '50%',
});
```

**Command Line Options:**
```bash
# Use default workers (all CPU cores)
npx playwright test

# Specific number of workers
npx playwright test --workers=4

# Sequential execution (no parallelization)
npx playwright test --workers=1

# Use 50% of CPU cores
npx playwright test --workers=50%
```

**Worker Scoped Fixtures:**
Share expensive setup across tests in the same worker:

```typescript
import { test as base } from '@playwright/test';

type WorkerFixtures = {
  database: Database;
  apiClient: APIClient;
};

export const test = base.extend<{}, WorkerFixtures>({
  // Worker-scoped: Setup once per worker, shared across tests
  database: [async ({}, use, workerInfo) => {
    console.log(`Worker ${workerInfo.workerIndex}: Setting up database`);
    
    const db = await setupDatabase();
    await db.seed();
    
    await use(db);
    
    console.log(`Worker ${workerInfo.workerIndex}: Tearing down database`);
    await db.close();
  }, { scope: 'worker' }],
  
  apiClient: [async ({}, use, workerInfo) => {
    const client = new APIClient(`worker-${workerInfo.workerIndex}`);
    await use(client);
    await client.dispose();
  }, { scope: 'worker' }],
});

// Usage in tests
test('test 1', async ({ database }) => {
  // Uses shared database from this worker
  const user = await database.getUser(1);
});

test('test 2', async ({ database }) => {
  // Same database instance as test 1 (if in same worker)
  const user = await database.getUser(2);
});
```

**Worker Information:**
Access worker info in tests:

```typescript
test('worker info', async ({ page }, testInfo) => {
  console.log('Worker index:', testInfo.workerIndex);  // 0, 1, 2, 3...
  console.log('Parallel index:', testInfo.parallelIndex);
  console.log('Project name:', testInfo.project.name);
});
```

**Serial Execution (Override Parallel):**
Run specific tests one after another:

```typescript
// All tests in this describe run serially
test.describe.serial('Dependent tests', () => {
  let orderId: string;
  
  test('create order', async ({ page }) => {
    // Must run first
    orderId = '12345';
  });
  
  test('update order', async ({ page }) => {
    // Runs after create (uses orderId)
    console.log('Updating:', orderId);
  });
  
  test('delete order', async ({ page }) => {
    // Runs after update
    console.log('Deleting:', orderId);
  });
});
```

**Parallel in Describe Block:**
```typescript
// Force parallel even if fullyParallel is false
test.describe.parallel('Independent tests', () => {
  test('test 1', async ({ page }) => {});
  test('test 2', async ({ page }) => {});
  test('test 3', async ({ page }) => {});
});
```

**Sharding (Distribute Across Machines):**
Split tests across multiple CI machines:

```bash
# Machine 1
npx playwright test --shard=1/3

# Machine 2
npx playwright test --shard=2/3

# Machine 3
npx playwright test --shard=3/3
```

Example GitHub Actions matrix:
```yaml
jobs:
  test:
    strategy:
      matrix:
        shardIndex: [1, 2, 3, 4]
        shardTotal: [4]
    steps:
      - run: npx playwright test --shard=${{ matrix.shardIndex }}/${{ matrix.shardTotal }}
```

**Benefits of Parallel Execution:**
- ✅ Faster test execution (4x with 4 workers)
- ✅ Better resource utilization
- ✅ Scales across multiple machines
- ✅ Isolated test execution

**Considerations:**
- ⚠️ Tests must be independent
- ⚠️ No shared global state
- ⚠️ Each test should clean up after itself
- ⚠️ More memory usage (multiple browsers)

**Best Practices:**
```typescript
// ✅ Good: Independent tests
test('create user', async ({ page }) => {
  const userId = `user-${Date.now()}`;  // Unique ID
  await createUser(userId);
  await cleanup(userId);  // Clean up
});

test('delete user', async ({ page }) => {
  const userId = `user-${Date.now()}`;  // Different unique ID
  await createUser(userId);
  await deleteUser(userId);
});

// ❌ Bad: Shared state
let globalUserId;  // Don't do this!

test('create user', async ({ page }) => {
  globalUserId = '123';  // Race condition!
});

test('delete user', async ({ page }) => {
  await deleteUser(globalUserId);  // May fail in parallel!
});
```

**Debugging with Workers:**
```bash
# Run with 1 worker for easier debugging
npx playwright test --workers=1 --headed

# See which worker ran which test
npx playwright test --reporter=line
```

**Performance Comparison:**
```bash
# Sequential (slow)
npx playwright test --workers=1
# Time: 120 seconds for 40 tests

# Parallel with 4 workers (4x faster)
npx playwright test --workers=4
# Time: 30 seconds for 40 tests

# All cores (optimal)
npx playwright test
# Time: 25 seconds for 40 tests (8 cores)
```

================================================================================

26. ADVANCED LEVEL QUESTIONS (Q41-Q60)
=======================================

Q41: How do you create custom fixtures in Playwright?
------------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Custom fixtures extend Playwright's built-in fixtures with your own reusable setup.

Basic custom fixture:
```typescript
// fixtures.ts
import { test as base } from '@playwright/test';

type MyFixtures = {
  authenticatedPage: Page;
};

export const test = base.extend<MyFixtures>({
  authenticatedPage: async ({ page }, use) => {
    // Setup: Login before test
    await page.goto('/login');
    await page.fill('#username', 'testuser');
    await page.fill('#password', 'password123');
    await page.click('#login');
    await page.waitForURL('/dashboard');
    
    // Provide to test
    await use(page);
    
    // Teardown: Logout after test (optional)
    await page.goto('/logout');
  },
});

export { expect } from '@playwright/test';
```

Use in test:
```typescript
import { test, expect } from './fixtures';

test('view profile', async ({ authenticatedPage }) => {
  // Already logged in!
  await authenticatedPage.goto('/profile');
  await expect(authenticatedPage.locator('.username')).toBeVisible();
});
```

Multiple custom fixtures:
```typescript
type MyFixtures = {
  adminPage: Page;
  userPage: Page;
  apiClient: APIRequestContext;
};

export const test = base.extend<MyFixtures>({
  adminPage: async ({ browser }, use) => {
    const context = await browser.newContext({ storageState: 'admin-auth.json' });
    const page = await context.newPage();
    await use(page);
    await context.close();
  },
  
  userPage: async ({ browser }, use) => {
    const context = await browser.newContext({ storageState: 'user-auth.json' });
    const page = await context.newPage();
    await use(page);
    await context.close();
  },
  
  apiClient: async ({ request }, use) => {
    const client = await request.newContext({
      baseURL: 'https://api.example.com',
      extraHTTPHeaders: {
        'Authorization': 'Bearer token123'
      }
    });
    await use(client);
    await client.dispose();
  },
});
```

Worker-scoped fixture (shared across tests):
```typescript
export const test = base.extend<{}, { database: Database }>({
  database: [async ({}, use) => {
    const db = await setupDatabase();
    await use(db);
    await db.close();
  }, { scope: 'worker' }],  // Created once per worker
});
```

Q42: What is the difference between page, context, and browser?
----------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Hierarchy: browser → context → page

1. Browser:
   - Top-level instance
   - Represents browser application (Chromium/Firefox/WebKit)
   - Expensive to create
   - Usually one per worker

2. Context (BrowserContext):
   - Isolated browser session
   - Has its own cookies, cache, localStorage
   - Like an incognito window
   - Multiple contexts per browser
   - Lightweight

3. Page:
   - Single tab/window
   - Multiple pages per context
   - Actual webpage you interact with

Relationships:
```typescript
// Manual creation
const browser = await chromium.launch();
const context = await browser.newContext();
const page = await context.newPage();

// Use page
await page.goto('https://example.com');

// Cleanup
await context.close();
await browser.close();
```

With Playwright Test (automatic):
```typescript
test('example', async ({ page, context, browser }) => {
  // Playwright provides all three automatically
  // page: Fresh page
  // context: Isolated context for this page
  // browser: Shared browser instance
});
```

When to use each:

Use page (most common):
```typescript
test('test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');  // 99% of tests
});
```

Use context (multiple pages):
```typescript
test('test', async ({ context }) => {
  const page1 = await context.newPage();
  const page2 = await context.newPage();
  
  await page1.goto('/page1');
  await page2.goto('/page2');
  
  // Share cookies/storage between pages
});
```

Use browser (isolated contexts):
```typescript
test('test', async ({ browser }) => {
  const context1 = await browser.newContext();
  const context2 = await browser.newContext();
  
  const page1 = await context1.newPage();
  const page2 = await context2.newPage();
  
  // Completely isolated (different cookies, storage)
});
```

Q43: How do you mock API responses in Playwright?
--------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Use route() to intercept and mock:

Mock single endpoint:
```typescript
await page.route('**/api/user', route => {
  route.fulfill({
    status: 200,
    contentType: 'application/json',
    body: JSON.stringify({
      id: 123,
      name: 'Test User',
      email: 'test@example.com'
    })
  });
});

await page.goto('/profile');  // Will use mocked data
```

Mock with different responses:
```typescript
await page.route('**/api/products', route => {
  if (route.request().method() === 'GET') {
    route.fulfill({
      status: 200,
      body: JSON.stringify([
        { id: 1, name: 'Product 1' },
        { id: 2, name: 'Product 2' }
      ])
    });
  } else if (route.request().method() === 'POST') {
    route.fulfill({
      status: 201,
      body: JSON.stringify({ id: 3, name: 'New Product' })
    });
  }
});
```

Mock error response:
```typescript
await page.route('**/api/data', route => {
  route.fulfill({
    status: 500,
    body: JSON.stringify({ error: 'Internal Server Error' })
  });
});
```

Mock from file:
```typescript
await page.route('**/api/products', route => {
  route.fulfill({
    path: './mocks/products.json'
  });
});
```

Abort request (block):
```typescript
await page.route('**/analytics/**', route => route.abort());
await page.route('**/*.{png,jpg,jpeg}', route => route.abort());  // Block images
```

Continue with modifications:
```typescript
await page.route('**/api/**', route => {
  route.continue({
    headers: {
      ...route.request().headers(),
      'Authorization': 'Bearer fake-token'
    }
  });
});
```

Conditional mocking:
```typescript
await page.route('**/api/user', route => {
  const url = route.request().url();
  if (url.includes('admin')) {
    route.fulfill({ body: JSON.stringify({ role: 'admin' }) });
  } else {
    route.continue();  // Use real API
  }
});
```

Remove route:
```typescript
const handler = route => route.fulfill({ body: 'mocked' });
await page.route('**/api/**', handler);

// Later: remove route
await page.unroute('**/api/**', handler);
```

Q44: What is the request fixture and how do you use it for API testing?
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:
The request fixture provides an APIRequestContext for API testing without a browser.

Basic API test:
```typescript
test('API: Get user', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/users/123');
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
  expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
  
  const data = await response.json();
  expect(data.name).toBe('John Doe');
  expect(data.email).toBe('john@example.com');
});
```

POST request:
```typescript
test('API: Create user', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.post('https://api.example.com/users', {
    data: {
      name: 'New User',
      email: 'new@example.com'
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.status()).toBe(201);
  const user = await response.json();
  expect(user.id).toBeDefined();
});
```

With headers:
```typescript
test('API: Authenticated request', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('https://api.example.com/profile', {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer token123',
      'Content-Type': 'application/json'
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

PUT/PATCH/DELETE:
```typescript
// Update
await request.put('https://api.example.com/users/123', {
  data: { name: 'Updated Name' }
});

// Partial update
await request.patch('https://api.example.com/users/123', {
  data: { email: 'newemail@example.com' }
});

// Delete
const response = await request.delete('https://api.example.com/users/123');
expect(response.status()).toBe(204);
```

Configure baseURL:
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    baseURL: 'https://api.example.com',
  },
});

// Test
test('API test', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('/users/123');  // Uses baseURL
});
```

Custom request context:
```typescript
test('API with custom context', async ({ playwright }) => {
  const apiContext = await playwright.request.newContext({
    baseURL: 'https://api.example.com',
    extraHTTPHeaders: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer token123',
      'X-API-Key': 'key123'
    }
  });
  
  const response = await apiContext.get('/users');
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
  
  await apiContext.dispose();
});
```

Response assertions:
```typescript
const response = await request.get('/users/123');

expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
expect(response.statusText()).toBe('OK');
expect(response.headers()['content-type']).toContain('application/json');

const body = await response.text();
const json = await response.json();
const buffer = await response.body();
```

Q45: How do you handle multiple tabs/windows in Playwright?
------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Wait for new page:
```typescript
test('open new tab', async ({ context }) => {
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  // Click link that opens new tab
  const [newPage] = await Promise.all([
    context.waitForEvent('page'),
    page.click('a[target="_blank"]')
  ]);
  
  // Interact with new tab
  await newPage.waitForLoadState();
  expect(await newPage.title()).toBe('New Page');
  
  // Switch back to original
  await page.bringToFront();
});
```

Multiple pages:
```typescript
test('multiple tabs', async ({ context }) => {
  const page1 = await context.newPage();
  const page2 = await context.newPage();
  
  await page1.goto('https://example.com/page1');
  await page2.goto('https://example.com/page2');
  
  // Interact with page1
  await page1.click('#button');
  
  // Interact with page2
  await page2.fill('#input', 'text');
});
```

Get all pages:
```typescript
test('all pages', async ({ context, page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  // Open new tabs
  await page.click('a[target="_blank"]');
  await page.click('a[target="_blank"]');
  
  // Get all pages
  const pages = context.pages();
  console.log(`Total pages: ${pages.length}`);
  
  for (const p of pages) {
    console.log(await p.title());
  }
});
```

Close specific page:
```typescript
const newPage = await context.newPage();
await newPage.goto('/page');
// ... interact
await newPage.close();
```

Popup handling:
```typescript
test('popup', async ({ page }) => {
  const [popup] = await Promise.all([
    page.waitForEvent('popup'),
    page.click('#open-popup')
  ]);
  
  await popup.waitForLoadState();
  await popup.fill('#input', 'text');
  await popup.click('#submit');
  
  await popup.close();
});
```

Q46: What is HAR (HTTP Archive) and how do you use it?
-------------------------------------------------------
Answer:
HAR files record network traffic for replay/inspection.

Record HAR:
```typescript
test('record network', async ({ browser }) => {
  const context = await browser.newContext({
    recordHar: { path: 'network.har' }
  });
  
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  await page.click('#button');
  
  await context.close();  // Saves HAR file
});
```

Replay HAR (mock all network):
```typescript
test('replay from HAR', async ({ browser }) => {
  const context = await browser.newContext({
    recordHar: { 
      path: 'network.har',
      mode: 'replay'  // Use recorded responses
    }
  });
  
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto('https://example.com');  // Uses HAR responses
});
```

Record specific URLs:
```typescript
const context = await browser.newContext({
  recordHar: {
    path: 'api.har',
    urlFilter: '**/api/**'  // Only record API calls
  }
});
```

HAR with routing:
```typescript
await page.routeFromHAR('network.har', {
  url: '**/api/**',  // Only replay API calls
  update: false  // Don't update HAR, only replay
});
```

Update mode (record if not in HAR):
```typescript
await page.routeFromHAR('network.har', {
  update: true  // Record new requests, replay existing
});
```

Use cases:
- Offline testing
- Consistent test data
- Reduce API calls
- Fast test execution
- Debugging network issues

Q47: How do you test responsive design and mobile viewports?
-------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Set viewport size:
```typescript
test.use({ viewport: { width: 375, height: 667 } });  // iPhone SE

test('mobile view', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  // Test mobile layout
});
```

Device emulation:
```typescript
import { devices } from '@playwright/test';

test.use(devices['iPhone 13 Pro']);

test('iPhone test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  // Full iPhone emulation (viewport, user agent, touch)
});
```

Available devices:
```typescript
devices['iPhone 13']
devices['iPhone 13 Pro']
devices['iPhone 13 Pro Max']
devices['Pixel 5']
devices['Galaxy S9+']
devices['iPad Pro']
devices['Desktop Chrome']
devices['Desktop Firefox']
```

Multiple viewports in config:
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    {
      name: 'mobile',
      use: devices['iPhone 13'],
    },
    {
      name: 'tablet',
      use: devices['iPad Pro'],
    },
    {
      name: 'desktop',
      use: { viewport: { width: 1920, height: 1080 } },
    },
  ],
});
```

Custom mobile configuration:
```typescript
test.use({
  viewport: { width: 375, height: 667 },
  userAgent: 'Mozilla/5.0 (iPhone; CPU iPhone OS 13_0 like Mac OS X)',
  hasTouch: true,
  isMobile: true,
  deviceScaleFactor: 2,
  locale: 'en-US',
});
```

Change viewport during test:
```typescript
test('responsive', async ({ page }) => {
  // Desktop
  await page.setViewportSize({ width: 1920, height: 1080 });
  await page.goto('/');
  await expect(page.locator('.desktop-nav')).toBeVisible();
  
  // Mobile
  await page.setViewportSize({ width: 375, height: 667 });
  await expect(page.locator('.mobile-menu')).toBeVisible();
  
  // Tablet
  await page.setViewportSize({ width: 768, height: 1024 });
  await expect(page.locator('.tablet-layout')).toBeVisible();
});
```

Touch events:
```typescript
test.use(devices['iPhone 13']);

test('touch gestures', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Tap
  await page.locator('#button').tap();
  
  // Swipe
  await page.touchscreen.swipe({ x: 100, y: 100 }, { x: 100, y: 500 });
});
```

Q48: How do you handle authentication tokens (JWT, Bearer tokens)?
-------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Set token in request headers:
```typescript
test('API with token', async ({ request }) => {
  const response = await request.get('/api/profile', {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9...'
    }
  });
  
  expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy();
});
```

Set token for all requests:
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    extraHTTPHeaders: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer YOUR_TOKEN'
    }
  }
});
```

Dynamic token from login:
```typescript
// auth.setup.ts
import { test as setup } from '@playwright/test';

setup('authenticate', async ({ request }) => {
  // Login to get token
  const response = await request.post('/api/login', {
    data: {
      username: 'testuser',
      password: 'password123'
    }
  });
  
  const { token } = await response.json();
  
  // Save token to environment
  process.env.AUTH_TOKEN = token;
  
  // Or save to file
  const fs = require('fs');
  fs.writeFileSync('auth.json', JSON.stringify({ token }));
});

// In tests
test('use token', async ({ request }) => {
  const token = process.env.AUTH_TOKEN;
  
  const response = await request.get('/api/data', {
    headers: { 'Authorization': `Bearer ${token}` }
  });
});
```

Token in localStorage:
```typescript
test('set token in localStorage', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await page.evaluate((token) => {
    localStorage.setItem('authToken', token);
  }, 'your-jwt-token');
  
  await page.reload();  // App reads token from localStorage
});
```

Token in cookies:
```typescript
test('set token cookie', async ({ context }) => {
  await context.addCookies([{
    name: 'auth_token',
    value: 'your-jwt-token',
    domain: 'example.com',
    path: '/',
    httpOnly: true,
    secure: true,
    sameSite: 'Strict'
  }]);
  
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto('/');  // Cookie sent automatically
});
```

Custom fixture with token:
```typescript
type Fixtures = {
  authenticatedRequest: APIRequestContext;
};

export const test = base.extend<Fixtures>({
  authenticatedRequest: async ({ playwright }, use) => {
    const context = await playwright.request.newContext({
      extraHTTPHeaders: {
        'Authorization': `Bearer ${process.env.AUTH_TOKEN}`
      }
    });
    await use(context);
    await context.dispose();
  }
});
```

Q49: What is visual regression testing and how do you implement it?
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:
Visual regression testing compares screenshots to detect unintended UI changes.

Basic screenshot comparison:
```typescript
test('visual regression', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('homepage.png');
});
```

First run:
- Takes screenshot
- Saves as baseline: `homepage.png`

Subsequent runs:
- Takes new screenshot
- Compares with baseline
- Fails if differences exceed threshold

Element screenshot:
```typescript
test('component visual test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  const header = page.locator('header');
  await expect(header).toHaveScreenshot('header.png');
});
```

With options:
```typescript
await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('page.png', {
  fullPage: true,                    // Capture entire page
  maxDiffPixels: 100,                // Allow 100 pixel difference
  maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.1,            // Allow 10% difference
  threshold: 0.2,                    // Color threshold (0-1)
  animations: 'disabled',            // Disable animations
  mask: [page.locator('.dynamic')],  // Mask dynamic content
  clip: { x: 0, y: 0, width: 800, height: 600 }
});
```

Mask dynamic content:
```typescript
test('mask timestamps', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/dashboard');
  
  await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('dashboard.png', {
    mask: [
      page.locator('.timestamp'),
      page.locator('.live-data'),
      page.locator('.random-ads')
    ]
  });
});
```

Cross-browser visual testing:
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    { name: 'chromium', use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] } },
    { name: 'firefox', use: { ...devices['Desktop Firefox'] } },
    { name: 'webkit', use: { ...devices['Desktop Safari'] } },
  ],
});
```

Each browser gets its own baseline:
- `homepage-chromium.png`
- `homepage-firefox.png`
- `homepage-webkit.png`

Update snapshots:
```
npx playwright test --update-snapshots
```

Inspect differences:
```
npx playwright show-report
```

Answer:

Set locale:

```typescript
test.use({ locale: 'de-DE' });

test('German locale', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
```

Set timezone:
```typescript
test.use({ 
  locale: 'en-US',
  timezoneId: 'America/New_York'
});

test('New York time', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  // Times displayed in EST/EDT
});
```

Multiple locales:
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    {
      name: 'English',

    },
    {
      name: 'German',
      use: { locale: 'de-DE' },

    {
      name: 'Japanese',
```

Test currency formatting:

```typescript
test.use({ locale: 'en-US' });

test('USD currency', async ({ page }) => {
```

```typescript
const testDate = new Date('2025-12-27T12:00:00Z');

  // Expect: 12/27/2025, 7:00 AM
```

```typescript
const context = await browser.newContext({
```
