Q51: How do you handle WebSockets in Playwright?
-------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Listen to WebSocket events:
```typescript
test('WebSocket messages', async ({ page }) => {
  // Listen for WebSocket creation
  page.on('websocket', ws => {
    console.log(`WebSocket opened: ${ws.url()}`);
    
    // Listen for frames sent to server
    ws.on('framesent', event => {
      console.log('Sent:', event.payload);
    });
    
    // Listen for frames received from server
    ws.on('framereceived', event => {
      console.log('Received:', event.payload);
    });
    
    // Listen for close
    ws.on('close', () => {
      console.log('WebSocket closed');
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('https://example.com/chat');
});
```

Wait for specific WebSocket message:
```typescript
test('wait for WS message', async ({ page }) => {
  const wsPromise = page.waitForEvent('websocket');
  await page.goto('/realtime');
  
  const ws = await wsPromise;
  
  // Wait for specific message
  const framePromise = new Promise(resolve => {
    ws.on('framereceived', event => {
      const data = JSON.parse(event.payload as string);
      if (data.type === 'connected') {
        resolve(data);
      }
    });
  });
  
  const connectedMsg = await framePromise;
  console.log('Connected:', connectedMsg);
});
```

Mock WebSocket responses:
```typescript
test('mock WebSocket', async ({ page }) => {
  page.on('websocket', ws => {
    ws.on('framesent', event => {
      const sent = JSON.parse(event.payload as string);
      
      // Mock response based on sent message
      if (sent.action === 'subscribe') {
        // Can't send frames directly, but can verify
        console.log('Subscription request received');
      }
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/live-updates');
});
```

Collect WebSocket messages:
```typescript
test('collect WS messages', async ({ page }) => {
  const messages: any[] = [];
  
  page.on('websocket', ws => {
    ws.on('framereceived', event => {
      messages.push(JSON.parse(event.payload as string));
    });
  });
  
  await page.goto('/trading');
  await page.waitForTimeout(5000);  // Collect for 5 seconds
  
  console.log(`Received ${messages.length} messages`);
  expect(messages.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
});
```

Test real-time updates:
```typescript
test('real-time chat', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/chat');
  
  const messagePromise = new Promise<string>(resolve => {
    page.on('websocket', ws => {
      ws.on('framereceived', event => {
        const data = JSON.parse(event.payload as string);
        if (data.type === 'message') {
          resolve(data.text);
        }
      });
    });
  });
  
  // Send message
  await page.fill('#message', 'Hello!');
  await page.click('#send');
  
  // Wait for echo/response
  const receivedMessage = await messagePromise;
  expect(receivedMessage).toContain('Hello');
});
```

Q52: How do you implement data-driven testing (parameterized tests)?
---------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Using test.describe with for loop:
```typescript
const users = [
  { username: 'user1', password: 'pass1', expected: 'User One' },
  { username: 'user2', password: 'pass2', expected: 'User Two' },
  { username: 'admin', password: 'admin123', expected: 'Administrator' },
];

for (const user of users) {
  test(`Login as ${user.username}`, async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/login');
    await page.fill('#username', user.username);
    await page.fill('#password', user.password);
    await page.click('#login');
    
    await expect(page.locator('.welcome')).toHaveText(`Welcome, ${user.expected}`);
  });
}
```

From CSV file:
```typescript
import fs from 'fs';
import { parse } from 'csv-parse/sync';

const records = parse(fs.readFileSync('testdata.csv'), {
  columns: true,
  skip_empty_lines: true
});

for (const record of records) {
  test(`Test: ${record.testName}`, async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto(record.url);
    await page.fill('#input', record.inputValue);
    await expect(page.locator('.result')).toHaveText(record.expectedResult);
  });
}
```

From JSON file:
```typescript
import testData from './testdata.json';

for (const data of testData.testCases) {
  test(`Search: ${data.searchTerm}`, async ({ page }) => {
    await page.goto('/search');
    await page.fill('#search', data.searchTerm);
    await page.click('#submit');
    
    await expect(page.locator('.results')).toHaveCount(data.expectedCount);
  });
}
```

Using test.describe.parallel:
```typescript
const browsers = ['Chrome', 'Firefox', 'Safari'];

test.describe.parallel('Cross-browser tests', () => {
  for (const browser of browsers) {
    test(`Test on ${browser}`, async ({ page }) => {
      // Test logic
    });
  }
});
```

With fixtures:
```typescript
const testUsers = [
  { role: 'admin', permissions: ['read', 'write', 'delete'] },
  { role: 'editor', permissions: ['read', 'write'] },
  { role: 'viewer', permissions: ['read'] },
];

for (const user of testUsers) {
  test(`${user.role} permissions`, async ({ page }) => {
    // Login as user.role
    await page.goto('/dashboard');
    
    for (const permission of user.permissions) {
      await expect(page.locator(`[data-permission="${permission}"]`)).toBeVisible();
    }
  });
}
```

Matrix testing:
```typescript
const browsers = ['chromium', 'firefox', 'webkit'];
const viewports = [
  { width: 1920, height: 1080 },
  { width: 375, height: 667 }
];

for (const browser of browsers) {
  for (const viewport of viewports) {
    test(`${browser} ${viewport.width}x${viewport.height}`, async ({ page }) => {
      await page.setViewportSize(viewport);
      // Test logic
    });
  }
}
```

Q53: How do you integrate Playwright with CI/CD (GitHub Actions)?
------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Basic GitHub Actions workflow:
```yaml
# .github/workflows/playwright.yml
name: Playwright Tests

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, master ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main, master ]

jobs:
  test:
    timeout-minutes: 60
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
      with:
        node-version: 18
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: npm ci
    
    - name: Install Playwright Browsers
      run: npx playwright install --with-deps
    
    - name: Run Playwright tests
      run: npx playwright test
    
    - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
      if: always()
      with:
        name: playwright-report
        path: playwright-report/
        retention-days: 30
```

With multiple browsers:
```yaml
jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      matrix:
        browser: [chromium, firefox, webkit]
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
    - run: npm ci
    - run: npx playwright install --with-deps
    - run: npx playwright test --project=${{ matrix.browser }}
```

With sharding (parallel execution):
```yaml
jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        shardIndex: [1, 2, 3, 4]
        shardTotal: [4]
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
    - run: npm ci
    - run: npx playwright install --with-deps
    - run: npx playwright test --shard=${{ matrix.shardIndex }}/${{ matrix.shardTotal }}
    
    - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
      if: always()
      with:
        name: playwright-report-${{ matrix.shardIndex }}
        path: playwright-report/
```

With environment variables:
```yaml
- name: Run tests
  env:
    BASE_URL: https://staging.example.com
    API_KEY: ${{ secrets.API_KEY }}
  run: npx playwright test
```

Deploy report to GitHub Pages:
```yaml
- name: Deploy report
  if: always()
  uses: peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v3
  with:
    github_token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
    publish_dir: ./playwright-report
```

Other CI platforms:

GitLab CI:
```yaml
# .gitlab-ci.yml
test:
  image: mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.40.0-focal
  script:
    - npm ci
    - npx playwright test
  artifacts:
    when: always
    paths:
      - playwright-report/
```

Jenkins:
```groovy
pipeline {
  agent any
  stages {
    stage('Test') {
      steps {
        sh 'npm ci'
        sh 'npx playwright install --with-deps'
        sh 'npx playwright test'
      }
    }
  }
  post {
    always {
      publishHTML(target: [
        reportDir: 'playwright-report',
        reportFiles: 'index.html',
        reportName: 'Playwright Report'
      ])
    }
  }
}
```

Q54: How do you handle dynamic content and race conditions?
------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Auto-waiting handles most cases:
```typescript
// Playwright waits automatically
await page.click('#button');  // Waits for button to be ready
await expect(page.locator('.result')).toBeVisible();  // Auto-retries
```

Wait for specific element state:
```typescript
// Wait for element to exist
await page.waitForSelector('.element');

// Wait for element to be visible
await page.waitForSelector('.element', { state: 'visible' });

// Wait for element to be hidden
await page.waitForSelector('.loading', { state: 'hidden' });

// Wait for element to be removed from DOM
await page.waitForSelector('.element', { state: 'detached' });
```

Wait for network idle:
```typescript
await page.goto('/dashboard');
await page.waitForLoadState('networkidle');
```

Wait for specific request/response:
```typescript
// Wait for API call
const responsePromise = page.waitForResponse('**/api/data');
await page.click('#load-data');
const response = await responsePromise;
expect(response.status()).toBe(200);

// Wait for request
const requestPromise = page.waitForRequest('**/api/submit');
await page.click('#submit');
await requestPromise;
```

Wait for function to return true:
```typescript
await page.waitForFunction(() => {
  return document.querySelectorAll('.item').length > 10;
});

// With arguments
await page.waitForFunction(
  (minCount) => document.querySelectorAll('.item').length >= minCount,
  10  // minCount = 10
);
```

Wait for URL change:
```typescript
await page.click('#next');
await page.waitForURL('**/page2');

// With regex
await page.waitForURL(/\/dashboard\/\d+/);

// Wait for URL to contain
await page.waitForURL('**/success**');
```

Retry custom logic:
```typescript
import { expect } from '@playwright/test';

await expect(async () => {
  const text = await page.locator('.dynamic').textContent();
  expect(text).toContain('Expected');
}).toPass({
  timeout: 10000,
  intervals: [100, 250, 500]  // Retry intervals
});
```

Handle race conditions:
```typescript
// ❌ Bad: Race condition
await page.click('#load');
await expect(page.locator('.result')).toBeVisible();  // Might fail

// ✓ Good: Wait for loading to start and finish
await page.click('#load');
await expect(page.locator('.loading')).toBeVisible();
await expect(page.locator('.loading')).toBeHidden();
await expect(page.locator('.result')).toBeVisible();
```

Promise.race for alternatives:
```typescript
const button = page.locator('#button');
const error = page.locator('.error');

await Promise.race([
  button.click(),
  expect(error).toBeVisible()
]);
```

Q55: What is the difference between soft and hard assertions?
--------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Hard assertions (default):
```typescript
test('hard assertions', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await expect(page.locator('.title')).toHaveText('Welcome');  // ❌ Fails here
  await expect(page.locator('.subtitle')).toBeVisible();       // ⏭️ Never runs
  await expect(page.locator('.footer')).toBeVisible();         // ⏭️ Never runs
});
```

Behavior: Test stops at first failure

Soft assertions:
```typescript
test('soft assertions', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  await expect.soft(page.locator('.title')).toHaveText('Welcome');     // ❌ Fails, continues
  await expect.soft(page.locator('.subtitle')).toBeVisible();          // ✓ Runs
  await expect.soft(page.locator('.footer')).toBeVisible();            // ✓ Runs
  
  // Test fails at the end if any soft assertion failed
});
```

Behavior: Collects all failures, reports at end

When to use soft assertions:
- Checking multiple independent conditions
- Visual regression tests
- Form validation tests
- Comprehensive page checks

Example: Form validation:
```typescript
test('form validation', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/form');
  await page.click('#submit');  // Submit empty form
  
  // Check all error messages (want to see all failures)
  await expect.soft(page.locator('#name-error')).toBeVisible();
  await expect.soft(page.locator('#email-error')).toBeVisible();
  await expect.soft(page.locator('#phone-error')).toBeVisible();
  await expect.soft(page.locator('#address-error')).toBeVisible();
});
```

Mix hard and soft:
```typescript
test('mixed assertions', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Hard: Must pass to continue
  await expect(page.locator('.login-form')).toBeVisible();
  
  await page.fill('#username', 'user');
  await page.fill('#password', 'pass');
  await page.click('#login');
  
  // Soft: Check all dashboard elements
  await expect.soft(page.locator('.welcome')).toBeVisible();
  await expect.soft(page.locator('.stats')).toBeVisible();
  await expect.soft(page.locator('.notifications')).toBeVisible();
});
```

Q56: How do you handle third-party cookies and CORS?
-----------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Set cookies:
```typescript
test('set cookies', async ({ context }) => {
  await context.addCookies([
    {
      name: 'session_id',
      value: 'abc123',
      domain: 'example.com',
      path: '/',
      httpOnly: true,
      secure: true,
      sameSite: 'Lax'
    },
    {
      name: 'tracking_id',
      value: 'xyz789',
      domain: '.example.com',  // All subdomains
      path: '/'
    }
  ]);
  
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
});
```

Get cookies:
```typescript
const cookies = await context.cookies();
console.log('All cookies:', cookies);

// Cookies for specific URL
const siteCookies = await context.cookies('https://example.com');
```

Clear cookies:
```typescript
await context.clearCookies();

// Clear specific cookies
await context.clearCookies({
  name: 'session_id',
  domain: 'example.com'
});
```

CORS handling:
Playwright automatically handles CORS - no extra configuration needed!

```typescript
// Works automatically
await page.goto('https://example.com');
const response = await page.request.get('https://api.different-domain.com/data');
// CORS not an issue in automation
```

Third-party contexts:
```typescript
test('third-party iframes', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  // Interact with third-party iframe (e.g., payment gateway)
  const paymentFrame = page.frameLocator('iframe[src*="payment-gateway.com"]');
  await paymentFrame.locator('#card-number').fill('4242424242424242');
  
  // Playwright handles cross-origin automatically
});
```

Bypass CSP (Content Security Policy):
```typescript
const context = await browser.newContext({
  bypassCSP: true  // Bypass Content-Security-Policy
});
```

Extra HTTP headers:
```typescript
const context = await browser.newContext({
  extraHTTPHeaders: {
    'X-Custom-Header': 'value',
    'Accept-Language': 'en-US'
  }
});
```

Q57: How do you test accessibility with Playwright?
----------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Install @axe-core/playwright:
```bash
npm install -D @axe-core/playwright
```

Basic accessibility test:
```typescript
import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test';
import AxeBuilder from '@axe-core/playwright';

test('accessibility check', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  
  const accessibilityScanResults = await new AxeBuilder({ page }).analyze();
  
  expect(accessibilityScanResults.violations).toEqual([]);
});
```

Check specific region:
```typescript
test('header accessibility', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const results = await new AxeBuilder({ page })
    .include('header')  // Only check header
    .analyze();
  
  expect(results.violations).toEqual([]);
});
```

Exclude elements:
```typescript
const results = await new AxeBuilder({ page })
  .exclude('.third-party-widget')  // Skip third-party content
  .exclude('#ads')
  .analyze();
```

Check specific rules:
```typescript
const results = await new AxeBuilder({ page })
  .withTags(['wcag2a', 'wcag2aa'])  // Only WCAG 2 Level A and AA
  .analyze();

// Or exclude specific rules
const results = await new AxeBuilder({ page })
  .disableRules(['color-contrast'])  // Skip color contrast check
  .analyze();
```

Detailed violation reporting:
```typescript
test('accessibility with details', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  const results = await new AxeBuilder({ page }).analyze();
  
  if (results.violations.length > 0) {
    console.log('Accessibility Violations:');
    results.violations.forEach(violation => {
      console.log(`\n${violation.id}: ${violation.description}`);
      console.log(`Impact: ${violation.impact}`);
      console.log(`Help: ${violation.helpUrl}`);
      violation.nodes.forEach(node => {
        console.log(`  Element: ${node.html}`);
        console.log(`  Fix: ${node.failureSummary}`);
      });
    });
  }
  
  expect(results.violations).toEqual([]);
});
```

Manual accessibility checks:
```typescript
test('keyboard navigation', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/form');
  
  // Tab through form
  await page.keyboard.press('Tab');
  await expect(page.locator('#name')).toBeFocused();
  
  await page.keyboard.press('Tab');
  await expect(page.locator('#email')).toBeFocused();
  
  await page.keyboard.press('Tab');
  await expect(page.locator('#submit')).toBeFocused();
});

test('ARIA labels', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/');
  
  // Check ARIA attributes
  await expect(page.locator('button[aria-label="Close"]')).toBeVisible();
  await expect(page.locator('[role="navigation"]')).toBeVisible();
  
  // Use accessible locators
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
  await page.getByLabel('Email address').fill('test@example.com');
});
```

Q58: How do you manage test dependencies and execution order?
--------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Serial execution (one after another):
```typescript
test.describe.serial('Dependent tests', () => {
  test('step 1: create account', async ({ page }) => {
    // Must run first
  });
  
  test('step 2: verify email', async ({ page }) => {
    // Runs after step 1
  });
  
  test('step 3: complete profile', async ({ page }) => {
    // Runs after step 2
  });
});
```

If any test fails, remaining tests are skipped.

Share state between tests:
```typescript
test.describe.serial('User journey', () => {
  let userId: string;
  
  test('create user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.post('/api/users', {
      data: { name: 'Test User' }
    });
    const data = await response.json();
    userId = data.id;  // Save for next test
  });
  
  test('update user', async ({ request }) => {
    await request.patch(`/api/users/${userId}`, {
      data: { email: 'newemail@example.com' }
    });
  });
  
  test('delete user', async ({ request }) => {
    const response = await request.delete(`/api/users/${userId}`);
    expect(response.status()).toBe(204);
  });
});
```

Global setup/teardown:
```typescript
// global-setup.ts
import { chromium, FullConfig } from '@playwright/test';

async function globalSetup(config: FullConfig) {
  // Runs once before all tests
  const browser = await chromium.launch();
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  
  // Setup: Create test data
  await page.goto('/admin');
  // ... create test data
  
  await browser.close();
}

export default globalSetup;

// global-teardown.ts
async function globalTeardown(config: FullConfig) {
  // Runs once after all tests
  // Cleanup test data
}

export default globalTeardown;
```

Configure in playwright.config.ts:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  globalSetup: require.resolve('./global-setup'),
  globalTeardown: require.resolve('./global-teardown'),
});
```

Project dependencies:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    {
      name: 'setup',
      testMatch: /.*\.setup\.ts/,
    },
    {
      name: 'chromium',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] },
      dependencies: ['setup'],  // Run 'setup' project first
    },
    {
      name: 'firefox',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Firefox'] },
      dependencies: ['setup'],
    },
  ],
});
```

Test execution order:
```typescript
// Tests run in alphabetical order within a file
test('01-first-test', async ({ page }) => {});
test('02-second-test', async ({ page }) => {});
test('03-third-test', async ({ page }) => {});
```

Worker-scoped fixtures (shared across tests):
```typescript
export const test = base.extend<{}, { workerData: any }>({
  workerData: [async ({}, use) => {
    // Setup: Runs once per worker
    const data = await setupExpensiveData();
    await use(data);
    // Teardown: Runs once per worker
    await cleanupData(data);
  }, { scope: 'worker' }],
});
```

Q59: How do you test forms with complex validation?
----------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Basic form test:
```typescript
test('form submission', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/signup');
  
  await page.fill('#name', 'John Doe');
  await page.fill('#email', 'john@example.com');
  await page.fill('#password', 'SecurePass123!');
  await page.fill('#confirm-password', 'SecurePass123!');
  
  await page.check('#terms');
  await page.selectOption('#country', 'USA');
  
  await page.click('#submit');
  
  await expect(page).toHaveURL('/welcome');
  await expect(page.locator('.success')).toHaveText('Account created!');
});
```

Test validation errors:
```typescript
test('form validation', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/signup');
  
  // Test empty form
  await page.click('#submit');
  await expect(page.locator('#name-error')).toHaveText('Name is required');
  await expect(page.locator('#email-error')).toHaveText('Email is required');
  
  // Test invalid email
  await page.fill('#email', 'invalid-email');
  await page.click('#submit');
  await expect(page.locator('#email-error')).toHaveText('Invalid email format');
  
  // Test password mismatch
  await page.fill('#password', 'Pass123!');
  await page.fill('#confirm-password', 'DifferentPass123!');
  await page.click('#submit');
  await expect(page.locator('#confirm-error')).toHaveText('Passwords must match');
});
```

Real-time validation:
```typescript
test('real-time validation', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/form');
  
  // Email validation as you type
  await page.fill('#email', 'test');
  await expect(page.locator('#email-error')).toHaveText('Invalid email');
  
  await page.fill('#email', 'test@example');
  await expect(page.locator('#email-error')).toHaveText('Invalid email');
  
  await page.fill('#email', 'test@example.com');
  await expect(page.locator('#email-error')).toBeHidden();
  await expect(page.locator('#email-success')).toBeVisible();
});
```

File upload validation:
```typescript
test('file upload validation', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/upload');
  
  // Test file size limit
  await page.setInputFiles('#file', 'large-file.pdf');
  await expect(page.locator('.error')).toHaveText('File too large (max 10MB)');
  
  // Test file type
  await page.setInputFiles('#file', 'document.txt');
  await expect(page.locator('.error')).toHaveText('Only PDF files allowed');
  
  // Test valid file
  await page.setInputFiles('#file', 'valid.pdf');
  await expect(page.locator('.success')).toBeVisible();
});
```

Multi-step form:
```typescript
test('multi-step form', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/register');
  
  // Step 1: Personal info
  await page.fill('#name', 'John');
  await page.fill('#email', 'john@example.com');
  await page.click('#next-step');
  
  // Verify step 2 appears
  await expect(page.locator('#step2')).toBeVisible();
  
  // Step 2: Address
  await page.fill('#address', '123 Main St');
  await page.fill('#city', 'Boston');
  await page.selectOption('#country', 'USA');
  await page.click('#next-step');
  
  // Step 3: Review
  await expect(page.locator('.review-name')).toHaveText('John');
  await expect(page.locator('.review-email')).toHaveText('john@example.com');
  await expect(page.locator('.review-address')).toHaveText('123 Main St');
  
  await page.click('#submit');
  await expect(page).toHaveURL('/success');
});
```

Dynamic fields:
```typescript
test('dynamic form fields', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/form');
  
  // Add dynamic field
  await page.click('#add-phone');
  await expect(page.locator('#phone-2')).toBeVisible();
  
  await page.click('#add-phone');
  await expect(page.locator('#phone-3')).toBeVisible();
  
  // Remove field
  await page.click('#remove-phone-2');
  await expect(page.locator('#phone-2')).toBeHidden();
  
  // Fill remaining fields
  await page.fill('#phone-1', '555-0001');
  await page.fill('#phone-3', '555-0003');
});
```

Q60: How do you handle test retries and flaky tests?
-----------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Configure retries globally:
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  retries: process.env.CI ? 2 : 0,  // 2 retries on CI, 0 locally
});
```

Per-project retries:
```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  projects: [
    {
      name: 'chromium',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] },
      retries: 1,
    },
    {
      name: 'webkit',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Safari'] },
      retries: 3,  // WebKit might need more retries
    },
  ],
});
```

Per-test retries:
```typescript
test('flaky test', async ({ page }) => {
  test.setTimeout(60000);  // 60 seconds timeout
  
  // Test logic that might be flaky
});

// Or use annotation
test('another test', {
  annotation: { type: 'flaky', description: 'Known flaky test' }
}, async ({ page }) => {
  // Test logic
});
```

Detect retry in test:
```typescript
test('retry-aware test', async ({ page }, testInfo) => {
  console.log(`Retry #${testInfo.retry}`);
  
  if (testInfo.retry > 0) {
    // Different behavior on retry
    await page.waitForTimeout(1000);
  }
  
  // Test logic
});
```

Avoid flaky tests:

❌ Bad practices:
```typescript
// Don't use arbitrary waits
await page.waitForTimeout(5000);

// Don't assume timing
await page.click('#button');
await page.locator('.result').textContent();  // Might not be ready
```

✓ Good practices:
```typescript
// Use auto-waiting
await page.click('#button');
await expect(page.locator('.result')).toBeVisible();

// Wait for specific conditions
await page.click('#load');
await page.waitForResponse('**/api/data');
await expect(page.locator('.loaded')).toBeVisible();

// Use stable locators
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();  // Better
await page.click('button:nth-child(3)');  // Fragile
```

Retry specific actions:
```typescript
await expect(async () => {
  await page.click('#intermittent-button');
  await expect(page.locator('.success')).toBeVisible();
}).toPass({
  intervals: [1000, 2000, 5000],  // Retry after 1s, 2s, 5s
  timeout: 10000
});
```

Mark known flaky tests:
```typescript
test.fixme('known flaky test', async ({ page }) => {
  // Will be skipped
});

test('conditional skip', async ({ page, browserName }) => {
  test.fixme(browserName === 'webkit', 'Flaky on Safari');
  // Test logic
});
```

Q61: How do you run only the tests that failed in the last run?
----------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

**Command:**
```bash
# Run all tests first
npx playwright test

# Then run only the failed tests
npx playwright test --last-failed
```

**How it works:**
- Playwright remembers which tests failed in the previous test run
- `--last-failed` flag filters and runs only those failed tests
- Very useful for quickly re-testing after fixes
- No need to manually specify test files or names

**Workflow Example:**
```bash
# Step 1: Run all tests
npx playwright test
# Output: 10 tests, 3 failed, 7 passed

# Step 2: Fix the bugs in your code

# Step 3: Run only the 3 failed tests
npx playwright test --last-failed
# Output: 3 tests, 3 passed ✓

# Step 4: Run full suite to confirm everything works
npx playwright test
```

**Combine with other flags:**
```bash
# Run last failed tests in headed mode
npx playwright test --last-failed --headed

# Run last failed tests in UI mode
npx playwright test --last-failed --ui

# Run last failed tests in debug mode
npx playwright test --last-failed --debug

# Run last failed tests on specific browser
npx playwright test --last-failed --project=chromium

# Run last failed tests with trace
npx playwright test --last-failed --trace on
```

**Use cases:**
1. ✅ **Quick iteration**: Fix bug → run failed tests → verify fix
2. ✅ **Save time**: No need to wait for all tests when debugging specific failures
3. ✅ **CI/CD**: Re-run only failed tests in retry stages
4. ✅ **Focus**: Concentrate on fixing failures without distraction

**Practical example:**
```bash
# Monday: Run test suite
npx playwright test
# 50 tests: 3 failed (login, checkout, payment)

# Fix login issue
# Run only failed tests
npx playwright test --last-failed
# 3 tests: 1 passed (login), 2 failed (checkout, payment)

# Fix checkout issue
npx playwright test --last-failed
# 2 tests: 1 passed (checkout), 1 failed (payment)

# Fix payment issue
npx playwright test --last-failed
# 1 test: 1 passed (payment) ✓

# Final verification - run all tests
npx playwright test
# 50 tests: 50 passed ✓
```

**Tips:**
- 💡 Failed test history is stored locally
- 💡 Running `npx playwright test` (without `--last-failed`) resets the history
- 💡 Works with any test configuration or project
- 💡 Combines with all other Playwright CLI flags

Q62: How do you view and use the HTML test report?
---------------------------------------------------
Answer:

**Automatic Opening:**
```bash
# Run tests
npx playwright test

# HTML report opens automatically if ANY tests failed
# If all tests pass, report is generated but not opened
```

**Manual Opening:**
```bash
# Open the HTML report manually
npx playwright show-report

# Open report from specific directory
npx playwright show-report playwright-report

# Open report from custom location
npx playwright show-report path/to/custom-report
```

**Configure HTML Reporter:**
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  reporter: [
    ['html', { 
      outputFolder: 'playwright-report',  // Default
      open: 'never',  // 'always', 'never', 'on-failure' (default)
    }]
  ],
});
```

**Report Configuration Options:**
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  reporter: [
    ['html', {
      outputFolder: 'my-report',
      open: 'always',           // Always open after test run
      // open: 'never',         // Never auto-open
      // open: 'on-failure',    // Open only if tests failed (default)
    }]
  ],
});
```

**HTML Report Features:**

**1. Main Dashboard:**
```
✅ Filter by status:
   - All tests
   - Passed tests
   - Failed tests
   - Flaky tests
   - Skipped tests

✅ Filter by browser:
   - Chromium
   - Firefox
   - WebKit
   - Mobile browsers

✅ Search functionality:
   - Search by test name
   - Search by file name
   - Instant filtering
```

**2. Test Details View:**
When you click on a test, you see:
```
📊 Test Information:
   - Test name and location
   - Duration
   - Browser/project
   - Retry count

📸 Screenshots:
   - Before failure
   - At failure point
   - After failure (if available)

🔍 Error Details:
   - Full error message
   - Stack trace
   - Expected vs actual values

📋 Test Steps:
   - Each action performed
   - Timing for each step
   - Screenshots for each step

🌐 Network Activity:
   - All network requests
   - Response status codes
   - Request/response data

📝 Console Logs:
   - Browser console output
   - Errors and warnings
   - Custom logs from test
```

**3. Attachments:**
```typescript
test('test with attachments', async ({ page }, testInfo) => {
  // Take screenshot
  const screenshot = await page.screenshot();
  await testInfo.attach('screenshot', { 
    body: screenshot, 
    contentType: 'image/png' 
  });
  
  // Attach text file
  await testInfo.attach('debug-info', {
    body: 'Some debug information',
    contentType: 'text/plain'
  });
  
  // Attach HTML
  await testInfo.attach('page-content', {
    body: await page.content(),
    contentType: 'text/html'
  });
});
```

All attachments appear in the HTML report under each test.

**4. Trace Files:**
If traces are enabled, the HTML report includes:
```
🎬 Trace Viewer:
   - Click "View Trace" for any test
   - Opens detailed trace in browser
   - See all actions, screenshots, DOM snapshots
   - Network activity timeline
```

**Multiple Reporters:**
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  reporter: [
    ['html'],                    // HTML report
    ['list'],                    // Console output
    ['json', { outputFile: 'results.json' }],  // JSON file
    ['junit', { outputFile: 'results.xml' }],  // JUnit XML
  ],
});
```

**Sharing Reports:**
```bash
# The HTML report is a static website
# You can:

# 1. Open locally
npx playwright show-report

# 2. Deploy to GitHub Pages
# - Push playwright-report/ folder to gh-pages branch

# 3. Deploy to any static hosting
# - Upload playwright-report/ folder to Netlify, Vercel, S3, etc.

# 4. Archive for CI/CD
# - Save as artifact in GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, etc.
```

**GitHub Actions Example:**
```yaml
- name: Run Playwright tests
  run: npx playwright test

- name: Upload HTML report
  if: always()
  uses: actions/upload-artifact@v3
  with:
    name: playwright-report
    path: playwright-report/
    retention-days: 30
```

**Report Statistics:**
```
The HTML report shows:
✓ Total tests run
✓ Pass/fail/skip counts
✓ Total duration
✓ Average test duration
✓ Flaky test percentage
✓ Browser-specific results
✓ Slowest tests
```

**Interactive Features:**
```
✅ Click test name → See full details
✅ Click screenshot → Enlarge view
✅ Click error → See full stack trace
✅ Click trace → Open trace viewer
✅ Use filters → Quickly find specific tests
✅ Use search → Find tests by name
✅ Click timestamps → See exact timing
```

**Command Summary:**
```bash
# Generate and open report
npx playwright test
npx playwright show-report

# Custom report location
npx playwright test --reporter=html:my-custom-report
npx playwright show-report my-custom-report

# Disable HTML report
npx playwright test --reporter=list

# Multiple reporters
npx playwright test --reporter=html,list,json
```

Q63: How do you run Playwright tests in VS Code?
-------------------------------------------------
Answer:

**Install VS Code Extension:**
```
1. Open VS Code
2. Go to Extensions (Ctrl+Shift+X or Cmd+Shift+X)
3. Search for "Playwright Test for VSCode"
4. Click "Install"

Or install via command line:
code --install-extension ms-playwright.playwright
```

**Features of VS Code Extension:**

**1. Testing Sidebar:**
```
✅ View all test files
✅ See test hierarchy (files → describe blocks → tests)
✅ Run/Debug buttons next to each test
✅ See test status (✓ passed, ✗ failed, ○ skipped)
✅ Filter tests by status
✅ Search tests by name
```

**2. Run Tests:**
```
Method 1: Click green triangle (▶) next to test
Method 2: Right-click test → "Run Test"
Method 3: Use Test Explorer sidebar
Method 4: Keyboard shortcuts
```

**Run Options:**
```
✅ Run single test
   - Click ▶ next to test() function

✅ Run all tests in describe block
   - Click ▶ next to test.describe()

✅ Run all tests in file
   - Click ▶ next to file name

✅ Run all tests in workspace
   - Click ▶ at top of Test Explorer

✅ Run last test
   - Uses VS Code's test history
```

**3. Debug Tests:**
```
Method 1: Click debug icon next to test (bug icon)
Method 2: Right-click test → "Debug Test"
Method 3: Set breakpoints, then debug

Debugging Features:
✓ Set breakpoints in test code
✓ Step through test execution
✓ Inspect variables
✓ View call stack
✓ Watch expressions
✓ Debug console
```

**4. Pick Locator:**
```
1. Click "Pick locator" button in Test Explorer
2. Browser opens with your app
3. Click any element on page
4. Locator code is generated
5. Copy locator to clipboard
6. Paste in your test
```

**5. Show Browser While Running:**
```
Command Palette (Ctrl+Shift+P):
> Playwright: Toggle headed mode

Or configure in settings.json:
{
  "playwright.headless": false
}
```

**6. View Test Output:**
```
✅ Test results appear in Test Explorer
✅ Click failed test → See error details
✅ View output panel for detailed logs
✅ See screenshots inline (if taken)
✅ Click "Show trace" → Opens trace viewer
```

**Extension Settings:**
```json
// .vscode/settings.json
{
  // Show browser while testing
  "playwright.headless": false,
  
  // Run tests in UI mode
  "playwright.useTestServer": true,
  
  // Playwright config file location
  "playwright.configPath": "playwright.config.ts",
  
  // Enable/disable extension
  "playwright.enabled": true,
  
  // Show trace viewer on failure
  "playwright.showTrace": true,
  
  // Reuse browser context (faster)
  "playwright.reuseBrowser": true
}
```

Q64: What are the different types of locators in Playwright?
-------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Playwright provides **8 built-in locators** that reflect how users interact with pages:

**1. getByRole() - RECOMMENDED (User-Facing)**
```typescript
// Buttons
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
await page.getByRole('button', { name: /sign in/i }).click();  // Regex

// Links
await page.getByRole('link', { name: 'Home' }).click();

// Checkboxes
await page.getByRole('checkbox', { name: 'Subscribe' }).check();

// Headings
await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Dashboard' })).toBeVisible();

// Text inputs (role: textbox)
await page.getByRole('textbox', { name: 'Username' }).fill('john');

// Select dropdowns (role: combobox)
await page.getByRole('combobox', { name: 'Country' }).selectOption('USA');

// Lists
await expect(page.getByRole('listitem')).toHaveCount(5);

// Tables
const table = page.getByRole('table');
const rows = page.getByRole('row');
```

**Common roles:**
- button, link, textbox, checkbox, radio, combobox, heading (h1-h6)
- listitem, table, row, cell, img, banner, navigation, main, dialog

**Why use getByRole?**
✅ Reflects how users and screen readers perceive the page
✅ Closest to accessibility standards
✅ Most resilient to code changes
✅ Follows W3C ARIA specifications

**2. getByLabel() - Form Controls**
```typescript
// Input with <label>
await page.getByLabel('Email Address').fill('test@example.com');
await page.getByLabel('Password').fill('secret');

// Works with aria-label too
await page.getByLabel('Search').fill('Playwright');

// Case-insensitive
await page.getByLabel('user name').fill('john');  // Matches "User Name"
```

**3. getByPlaceholder() - Input Hints**
```typescript
await page.getByPlaceholder('name@example.com').fill('test@test.com');
await page.getByPlaceholder('Enter your email').type('user@domain.com');
await page.getByPlaceholder(/search/i).fill('Playwright');
```

**4. getByText() - Text Content**
```typescript
// Exact match
await expect(page.getByText('Welcome, John')).toBeVisible();

// Substring match (default)
await page.getByText('Welcome').click();

// Exact match (explicit)
await page.getByText('Welcome', { exact: true }).click();

// Regex match
await expect(page.getByText(/welcome, [a-z]+/i)).toBeVisible();

// Works with partial text
await page.getByText('Add to Cart').click();  // Matches "Add to Cart"
```

**5. getByTestId() - Test Identifiers**
```typescript
// Default: data-testid attribute
await page.getByTestId('submit-button').click();
await page.getByTestId('user-profile').hover();

// Custom test id attribute
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    testIdAttribute: 'data-pw'  // Use data-pw instead
  }
});

// Then in HTML: <button data-pw="submit">Submit</button>
await page.getByTestId('submit').click();
```

**When to use testId:**
- Most resilient to changes
- Works when role/text is not available
- Good for dynamic content
- QA team can add test ids to HTML

**6. getByAltText() - Images**
```typescript
await page.getByAltText('Playwright logo').click();
await expect(page.getByAltText('Product image')).toBeVisible();
await page.getByAltText(/company logo/i).hover();
```

**7. getByTitle() - Title Attribute**
```typescript
await expect(page.getByTitle('Issues count')).toHaveText('25 issues');
await page.getByTitle('Settings').click();
```

**8. getByPlaceholder() - Input Placeholders**
```typescript
await page.getByPlaceholder('Search...').fill('Playwright');
await page.getByPlaceholder('Type your message').type('Hello');
```

**CSS and XPath Locators (NOT Recommended)**
```typescript
// CSS selectors
await page.locator('css=button.primary').click();
await page.locator('button').click();  // Auto-detected as CSS

// XPath selectors
await page.locator('xpath=//button').click();
await page.locator('//button').click();  // Auto-detected as XPath

// Why NOT recommended:
// ❌ Tied to DOM structure
// ❌ Break when HTML changes
// ❌ Not user-facing
// ❌ Hard to maintain

// Example of fragile selector:
await page.locator('#tsf > div:nth-child(2) > div.A8SBwf > div.RNNXgb').click();
```

**Locator Priority (Best Practice):**
```
1st: getByRole()           → User-facing, accessible
2nd: getByLabel()          → For form inputs
3rd: getByPlaceholder()    → For inputs without labels
4th: getByText()           → For non-interactive elements
5th: getByTestId()         → When above don't work
Last: CSS/XPath            → Avoid if possible
```

**Chaining Locators:**
```typescript
// Find button inside a specific div
await page
  .locator('.product-card')
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Add to Cart' })
  .click();

// Find heading inside dialog
await page
  .getByTestId('settings-dialog')
  .getByRole('heading', { name: 'Settings' })
  .click();
```

**Shadow DOM Support:**
```typescript
// Playwright pierces shadow DOM automatically
await page.getByText('Details').click();  // Works even in shadow DOM
// Exception: XPath doesn't pierce shadow roots
```

**Locator Comparison:**
```typescript
// ✓ GOOD - User-facing
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click();
await page.getByLabel('Email').fill('test@example.com');

// ⚠️ OK - When role/label not available
await page.getByTestId('submit-button').click();
await page.getByText('Add to Cart').click();

// ❌ BAD - Fragile, not user-facing
await page.locator('#app > div > button:nth-child(3)').click();
await page.locator('xpath=//div[@id="app"]//button[3]').click();
```

Q65: How do you filter and chain locators in Playwright?
---------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

**Filtering Locators:**

**1. Filter by Text (hasText)**
```typescript
// Filter list items by text
await page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .filter({ hasText: 'Product 2' })
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Add to Cart' })
  .click();

// Filter with regex
await page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .filter({ hasText: /Product \d+/ })
  .first()
  .click();
```

**2. Filter by NOT Having Text (hasNotText)**
```typescript
// Get items NOT out of stock
await expect(
  page.getByRole('listitem').filter({ hasNotText: 'Out of stock' })
).toHaveCount(5);

// Exclude specific items
await page
  .getByRole('button')
  .filter({ hasNotText: 'Cancel' })
  .click();
```

**3. Filter by Child/Descendant (has)**
```typescript
// Find list item containing specific heading
await page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .filter({ has: page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Product 2' }) })
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Add to Cart' })
  .click();

// Verify count
await expect(
  page
    .getByRole('listitem')
    .filter({ has: page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Product 2' }) })
).toHaveCount(1);
```

**4. Filter by NOT Having Child/Descendant (hasNot)**
```typescript
// Get list items NOT containing "Product 2"
await expect(
  page
    .getByRole('listitem')
    .filter({ hasNot: page.getByText('Product 2') })
).toHaveCount(1);
```

**5. Filter by Visibility**
```typescript
// Only visible elements
await page.locator('button').filter({ visible: true }).click();

// Invisible elements
const hiddenButtons = await page.locator('button').filter({ visible: false });
```

**Chaining Filters:**
```typescript
// Multiple filters
const rowLocator = page.getByRole('listitem');

await rowLocator
  .filter({ hasText: 'Mary' })
  .filter({ has: page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Say goodbye' }) })
  .screenshot({ path: 'screenshot.png' });

// Find specific product
await page
  .getByRole('article')
  .filter({ hasText: 'Category: Electronics' })
  .filter({ hasNot: page.getByText('Out of Stock') })
  .filter({ has: page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Buy Now' }) })
  .first()
  .click();
```

**Locator Operators:**

**1. Matching Inside a Locator (Chaining)**
```typescript
const product = page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .filter({ hasText: 'Product 2' });

await product.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add to Cart' }).click();
await expect(product).toHaveCount(1);

// Chain two locators
const saveButton = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Save' });
const dialog = page.getByTestId('settings-dialog');
await dialog.locator(saveButton).click();
```

**2. Matching Two Locators Simultaneously (and)**
```typescript
// Match by role AND title
const button = page
  .getByRole('button')
  .and(page.getByTitle('Subscribe'));

await button.click();

// Match button with specific class AND text
await page
  .locator('button')
  .and(page.getByText('Submit'))
  .click();
```

**3. Matching Alternative Locators (or)**
```typescript
// Click either "New email" button OR "Dismiss" in dialog
const newEmail = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'New' });
const dialog = page.getByText('Confirm security settings');

await expect(newEmail.or(dialog).first()).toBeVisible();

if (await dialog.isVisible()) {
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Dismiss' }).click();
}
await newEmail.click();

// Handle multiple possible states
await page
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Accept' })
  .or(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'OK' }))
  .or(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Confirm' }))
  .first()
  .click();
```

**Working with Lists:**

**1. Count Items**
```typescript
// Assert count
await expect(page.getByRole('listitem')).toHaveCount(3);

// Get count
const count = await page.getByRole('listitem').count();
console.log(`Found ${count} items`);
```

**2. Assert All Text**
```typescript
await expect(page.getByRole('listitem')).toHaveText([
  'apple',
  'banana',
  'orange'
]);
```

**3. Get Specific Item:**

**By Text:**
```typescript
await page.getByText('orange').click();
```

**By Filter:**
```typescript
await page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .filter({ hasText: 'orange' })
  .click();
```

**By Test ID:**
```typescript
await page.getByTestId('orange').click();
```

**By nth Index:**
```typescript
// First, last, nth
await page.getByRole('listitem').first().click();
await page.getByRole('listitem').last().click();
await page.getByRole('listitem').nth(1).click();  // Second item (0-indexed)

// Get all
const items = await page.getByRole('listitem').all();
for (const item of items) {
  console.log(await item.textContent());
}
```

**4. Iterate Over List:**
```typescript
// Using .all()
for (const row of await page.getByRole('listitem').all()) {
  console.log(await row.textContent());
}

// Using regular loop
const rows = page.getByRole('listitem');
const count = await rows.count();
for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
  console.log(await rows.nth(i).textContent());
}

// Evaluate in page
const texts = await page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .evaluateAll(list => list.map(el => el.textContent));
```

**Strictness Mode:**
```typescript
// ❌ Throws error if multiple buttons exist
await page.getByRole('button').click();
// Error: strict mode violation: locator resolved to 3 elements

// ✓ Works - operations that expect multiple elements
await page.getByRole('button').count();  // OK
await page.getByRole('button').all();    // OK

// ✓ Use first/last/nth to opt-out of strictness
await page.getByRole('button').first().click();
await page.getByRole('button').last().click();
await page.getByRole('button').nth(2).click();
```

**Practical Examples:**

**E-commerce Product Filtering:**
```typescript
// Find "Laptop" product that's in stock and on sale
await page
  .getByRole('article')
  .filter({ hasText: 'Laptop' })
  .filter({ hasNotText: 'Out of Stock' })
  .filter({ has: page.getByText('Sale') })
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Add to Cart' })
  .click();
```

**Form with Multiple Sections:**
```typescript
// Fill input in "Billing Address" section
await page
  .getByRole('region', { name: 'Billing Address' })
  .getByLabel('Street')
  .fill('123 Main St');

// Fill input in "Shipping Address" section
await page
  .getByRole('region', { name: 'Shipping Address' })
  .getByLabel('Street')
  .fill('456 Oak Ave');
```

**Table Cell Filtering:**
```typescript
// Click edit button in row containing "John Doe"
await page
  .getByRole('row')
  .filter({ hasText: 'John Doe' })
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Edit' })
  .click();

// Get all rows with status "Active"
const activeRows = await page
  .getByRole('row')
  .filter({ hasText: 'Active' })
  .all();
```

**Best Practices:**
```typescript
// ✓ GOOD - Specific, resilient
await page
  .getByTestId('product-list')
  .getByRole('button', { name: 'Buy' })
  .first()
  .click();

// ✓ GOOD - Filter by meaningful criteria
await page
  .getByRole('listitem')
  .filter({ hasText: 'Premium' })
  .filter({ hasNotText: 'Sold Out' })
  .click();

// ❌ BAD - Too generic, prone to strictness errors
await page.getByRole('button').click();

// ❌ BAD - Relies on position (fragile)
await page.getByRole('button').nth(5).click();
```

Q66: What are the different types of assertions in Playwright?
---------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

Playwright has **two types of assertions:**

**AUTO-RETRYING ASSERTIONS (Web-Specific)**
-------------------------------------------

**Characteristics:**
- ✅ Retry until condition is met or timeout (default 5 seconds)
- ✅ Must use `await`
- ✅ Re-fetch element on each retry
- ✅ Perfect for dynamic web content
- ✅ Handle auto-waiting automatically

**1. Visibility Assertions:**
```typescript
// Element is visible
await expect(page.getByText('Welcome')).toBeVisible();

// Element is hidden
await expect(page.getByText('Error')).toBeHidden();
await expect(page.getByText('Loading')).not.toBeVisible();

// Element is in viewport
await expect(page.getByRole('banner')).toBeInViewport();
```

**2. State Assertions:**
```typescript
// Checkbox/radio
await expect(page.getByLabel('Subscribe')).toBeChecked();
await expect(page.getByLabel('Subscribe')).not.toBeChecked();

// Enabled/disabled
await expect(page.getByRole('button')).toBeEnabled();
await expect(page.getByRole('button')).toBeDisabled();

// Editable
await expect(page.getByLabel('Email')).toBeEditable();

// Focused
await expect(page.getByLabel('Username')).toBeFocused();

// Attached to DOM
await expect(page.getByText('Hello')).toBeAttached();

// Empty (container)
await expect(page.locator('.error-messages')).toBeEmpty();
```

**3. Text Assertions:**
```typescript
// Exact text match
await expect(page.getByTestId('status')).toHaveText('Success');

// Exact text match (array for multiple elements)
await expect(page.getByRole('listitem')).toHaveText([
  'apple',
  'banana',
  'orange'
]);

// Contains text (substring)
await expect(page.locator('.message')).toContainText('Success');
await expect(page.locator('.message')).toContainText(/success/i);

// Regex match
await expect(page.locator('h1')).toHaveText(/Welcome, \w+/);

// NOT contain text
await expect(page.locator('.error')).not.toContainText('Failed');
```

**4. Attribute Assertions:**
```typescript
// Has attribute with value
await expect(page.locator('button')).toHaveAttribute('type', 'submit');
await expect(page.locator('a')).toHaveAttribute('href', '/home');
await expect(page.locator('img')).toHaveAttribute('src', /logo\.png/);

// Has ID
await expect(page.locator('div')).toHaveId('main-container');

// Has CSS class
await expect(page.locator('button')).toHaveClass('btn btn-primary');
await expect(page.locator('button')).toHaveClass(/btn-/);

// Contains CSS class
await expect(page.locator('button')).toContainClass('active');
await expect(page.locator('button')).toContainClass(['btn', 'primary']);

// Has CSS property
await expect(page.locator('button')).toHaveCSS('color', 'rgb(255, 0, 0)');
await expect(page.locator('button')).toHaveCSS('background-color', '#ff0000');

// Has JavaScript property
await expect(page.locator('input')).toHaveJSProperty('value', 'hello');
```

**5. Form Input Assertions:**
```typescript
// Input value
await expect(page.getByLabel('Email')).toHaveValue('test@example.com');
await expect(page.getByLabel('Email')).toHaveValue(/test@.+/);

// Empty value
await expect(page.getByLabel('Search')).toHaveValue('');

// Select dropdown (single)
await expect(page.locator('select')).toHaveValue('option1');

// Select dropdown (multiple)
await expect(page.locator('select[multiple]')).toHaveValues([
  'option1',
  'option2'
]);
```

**6. Count Assertions:**
```typescript
await expect(page.getByRole('listitem')).toHaveCount(5);
await expect(page.locator('.product')).toHaveCount(10);
await expect(page.getByRole('button')).toHaveCount(3);
```

**7. Accessibility Assertions:**
```typescript
// Accessible name
await expect(page.getByRole('button')).toHaveAccessibleName('Submit Form');

// Accessible description
await expect(page.getByRole('button')).toHaveAccessibleDescription(
  'Click to submit the form'
);

// ARIA role
await expect(page.locator('button')).toHaveRole('button');
```

**8. Screenshot Assertions (Visual Regression):**
```typescript
// Element screenshot
await expect(page.getByTestId('header')).toHaveScreenshot('header.png');

// Page screenshot
await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('home-page.png');

// With options
await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot('page.png', {
  maxDiffPixels: 100,           // Allow 100 pixels difference
  threshold: 0.2,               // 20% difference threshold
  animations: 'disabled',       // Disable animations
  mask: [page.locator('.ad')],  // Mask elements
});
```

**9. URL and Title Assertions:**
```typescript
// Page URL
await expect(page).toHaveURL('https://example.com/dashboard');
await expect(page).toHaveURL(/dashboard/);
await expect(page).toHaveURL(/.*\/dashboard$/);

// Page title
await expect(page).toHaveTitle('Dashboard - My App');
await expect(page).toHaveTitle(/Dashboard/);
```

**10. API Response Assertions:**
```typescript
// Response is OK (status 200-299)
const response = await page.request.get('/api/user');
await expect(response).toBeOK();
```

**11. ARIA Snapshot Assertions:**
```typescript
await expect(page.locator('body')).toMatchAriaSnapshot(`
  - heading "Login"
  - textbox "Username"
  - textbox "Password"
  - button "Sign in"
`);
```

**NON-RETRYING ASSERTIONS (Generic)**
--------------------------------------

**Characteristics:**
- ❌ Do NOT retry - evaluate immediately
- ❌ No `await` needed (but can use await)
- ❌ Can lead to flaky tests
- ✅ Use for non-web values (numbers, strings, booleans)

**1. Equality:**
```typescript
expect(value).toBe(10);                    // Strict equality (===)
expect(value).toEqual({ name: 'John' });   // Deep equality
expect(value).toStrictEqual({ id: 1 });    // Deep + type equality
```

**2. Truthiness:**
```typescript
expect(value).toBeTruthy();     // true, 1, "text", [], {}
expect(value).toBeFalsy();      // false, 0, "", null, undefined
expect(value).toBeDefined();    // not undefined
expect(value).toBeUndefined();  // undefined
expect(value).toBeNull();       // null
expect(value).toBeNaN();        // NaN
```

**3. Numbers:**
```typescript
expect(value).toBeGreaterThan(5);
expect(value).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(5);
expect(value).toBeLessThan(10);
expect(value).toBeLessThanOrEqual(10);
expect(value).toBeCloseTo(3.14, 2);  // Approximately (decimals)
```

**4. Strings:**
```typescript
expect(str).toContain('hello');         // Substring
expect(str).toMatch(/hello \w+/);       // Regex match
expect(str).toHaveLength(10);           // Length
```

**5. Arrays/Iterables:**
```typescript
expect(array).toContain('item');              // Contains element
expect(array).toContainEqual({ id: 1 });      // Contains similar object
expect(array).toHaveLength(5);                // Length
expect(array).toEqual([1, 2, 3]);             // Deep equality
```

**6. Objects:**
```typescript
expect(obj).toHaveProperty('name');
expect(obj).toHaveProperty('age', 25);
expect(obj).toMatchObject({ name: 'John' });  // Partial match
expect(obj).toEqual({ name: 'John', age: 25 });  // Exact match
```

**7. Exceptions:**
```typescript
expect(() => {
  throw new Error('Boom!');
}).toThrow();

expect(() => {
  throw new Error('Boom!');
}).toThrow('Boom!');

expect(() => {
  throw new Error('Boom!');
}).toThrow(/boom/i);
```

**8. Instances:**
```typescript
expect(obj).toBeInstanceOf(Array);
expect(obj).toBeInstanceOf(Date);
expect(obj).toBeInstanceOf(MyClass);
```

**ADVANCED ASSERTION TECHNIQUES**
----------------------------------

**1. Negation (.not)**
```typescript
await expect(page.getByText('Error')).not.toBeVisible();
expect(value).not.toEqual(0);
await expect(page).not.toHaveURL(/login/);
```

**2. Soft Assertions (Don't Stop on Failure)**
```typescript
// Test continues even if assertion fails
await expect.soft(page.getByTestId('status')).toHaveText('Success');
await expect.soft(page.getByTestId('eta')).toHaveText('1 day');

// Continue test
await page.getByRole('link', { name: 'next page' }).click();
await expect.soft(page.getByRole('heading')).toBeVisible();

// Check if any soft assertions failed
expect(test.info().errors).toHaveLength(0);
```

**3. Custom Expect Message**
```typescript
await expect(
  page.getByText('Name'),
  'User should be logged in'
).toBeVisible();

// On failure:
// Error: User should be logged in
//   Expected: visible
//   Received: hidden
```

**4. Custom Timeout**
```typescript
// Per assertion
await expect(page.getByText('Slow loading')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 10000 });

// Configure globally
const slowExpect = expect.configure({ timeout: 10000 });
await slowExpect(locator).toHaveText('Submit');
```

**5. Polling Assertions (expect.poll)**
```typescript
// Poll until condition is met
await expect.poll(async () => {
  const response = await page.request.get('/api/status');
  return response.status();
}, {
  message: 'API should return 200',
  timeout: 10000,
  intervals: [1000, 2000, 5000]  // Retry intervals
}).toBe(200);
```

**6. Retry Blocks (expect.toPass)**
```typescript
await expect(async () => {
  const response = await page.request.get('/api/data');
  expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
  const data = await response.json();
  expect(data.items).toHaveLength(5);
}).toPass({
  intervals: [1000, 2000, 5000],
  timeout: 10000
});
```

**Assertion Timeout Configuration:**
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  expect: {
    timeout: 5000,  // Default: 5 seconds
    toHaveScreenshot: {
      maxDiffPixels: 10,
    },
    toMatchSnapshot: {
      maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.1,
    },
  },
});
```

**Common Mistakes:**
```typescript
// ❌ BAD - Non-retrying assertion on web element
const text = await page.locator('.status').textContent();
expect(text).toBe('Success');  // Can be flaky!

// ✓ GOOD - Auto-retrying assertion
await expect(page.locator('.status')).toHaveText('Success');

// ❌ BAD - Forgot await
expect(page.locator('.status')).toHaveText('Success');  // Won't work!

// ✓ GOOD - With await
await expect(page.locator('.status')).toHaveText('Success');
```

**Assertion Cheat Sheet:**
```typescript
// Visibility
await expect(locator).toBeVisible()
await expect(locator).toBeHidden()

// Text
await expect(locator).toHaveText('exact')
await expect(locator).toContainText('partial')

// Input
await expect(locator).toHaveValue('value')

// Count
await expect(locator).toHaveCount(5)

// State
await expect(locator).toBeChecked()
await expect(locator).toBeEnabled()
await expect(locator).toBeDisabled()

// Page
await expect(page).toHaveURL(/dashboard/)
await expect(page).toHaveTitle('Title')

// Generic
expect(value).toBe(10)
expect(value).toEqual(obj)
expect(array).toContain('item')
```

Q67: Explain Playwright's architecture and how it works internally?
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Answer:

**ARCHITECTURE OVERVIEW:**

Playwright uses a **three-layer architecture** for browser automation:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│     Layer 1: Test Code              │  ← Your TypeScript/JavaScript
│     (Node.js Environment)           │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────┘
               │ Playwright API
               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│     Layer 2: Playwright Library     │  ← Core automation engine
│     (Node.js Package)               │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────┘
               │ Browser Protocols
               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│     Layer 3: Browser Binaries       │  ← Chromium, Firefox, WebKit
│     (Actual Browsers)               │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Reference URLs for Architecture Diagrams:**
```
📚 Official Architecture Documentation:
https://playwright.dev/docs/library

📚 Browser Contexts Architecture:
https://playwright.dev/docs/browser-contexts

📚 GitHub Technical Overview:
https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/blob/main/docs/src/library.md

📚 Why Playwright (Architecture Benefits):
https://playwright.dev/docs/why-playwright
```

**LAYER 1: TEST CODE (Your Code)**
-----------------------------------

**What it is:**
- Your test files written in JavaScript/TypeScript
- Runs in Node.js environment (NOT in browser)
- Uses Playwright API to control browsers

**Example:**
```typescript
import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test';

test('login test', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('https://example.com');
  await page.fill('#username', 'user');
  await page.click('#submit');
  await expect(page).toHaveURL('/dashboard');
});
```

**Key Points:**
- Executes in Node.js (V8 JavaScript engine)
- Has access to Node.js APIs (filesystem, network, etc.)
- Communicates with Playwright library via API calls
- Supports async/await for handling asynchronous operations

**LAYER 2: PLAYWRIGHT LIBRARY (Core Engine)**
----------------------------------------------

**Components:**

**1. Test Runner (@playwright/test)**
```typescript
Responsibilities:
├── Test discovery (finding *.spec.ts files)
├── Parallel execution (managing workers)
├── Fixture management (providing page, context, browser)
├── Hooks (beforeEach, afterEach, beforeAll, afterAll)
├── Reporting (HTML, JSON, JUnit, etc.)
└── Retry logic (re-running failed tests)
```

**2. Core Library (playwright)**
```typescript
Responsibilities:
├── Browser launching and management
├── Context creation and isolation
├── Page automation and control
├── Locator engine and selectors
├── Auto-waiting and retry logic
├── Network interception
├── Screenshot and video recording
└── Trace generation
```

**How It Works Internally:**

**Browser Management:**
```typescript
// When you run tests
1. Playwright downloads browser binaries (if not present)
   Location: node_modules/playwright/.local-browsers/
   
2. Launches browser process with specific arguments
   Example: chromium --headless --disable-gpu
   
3. Establishes WebSocket connection to browser
   Protocol: DevTools Protocol (Chromium)
             Juggler Protocol (Firefox)
             WebKit Protocol (WebKit)
```

**Context Management:**
```typescript
// Playwright creates contexts automatically
Browser (One per worker)
└── Context 1 (Test 1) → Isolated session
    ├── Own cookies
    ├── Own storage
    ├── Own cache
    └── Page 1

└── Context 2 (Test 2) → Isolated session
    ├── Own cookies
    ├── Own storage
    ├── Own cache
    └── Page 2
```

**Why Contexts Are Important:**
- Lightweight (milliseconds to create vs seconds for browser)
- Complete isolation between tests
- No test pollution
- Safe parallel execution
- Like incognito mode for each test

**LAYER 3: BROWSER COMMUNICATION**
-----------------------------------

**Three Different Browser Protocols:**

**1. Chromium - Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)**
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Playwright Library                 │
│          ↓                          │
│  WebSocket Connection               │
│          ↓                          │
│  Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)     │
│          ↓                          │
│  Chromium Browser                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘

Features:
✓ Industry-standard protocol
✓ Same protocol Chrome DevTools uses
✓ Rich debugging capabilities
✓ Mature and well-documented
```

**2. Firefox - Juggler Protocol (Custom)**
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Playwright Library                 │
│          ↓                          │
│  WebSocket Connection               │
│          ↓                          │
│  Juggler Protocol (Playwright)      │
│          ↓                          │
│  Firefox Browser (Modified)         │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘

Features:
✓ Built by Playwright team
✓ Integrated into Firefox
✓ Mirrors CDP capabilities
✓ Ensures API consistency
```

**3. WebKit - WebKit Protocol (Custom)**
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Playwright Library                 │
│          ↓                          │
│  WebSocket Connection               │
│          ↓                          │
│  WebKit Protocol (Playwright)       │
│          ↓                          │
│  WebKit Browser                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘

Features:
✓ Custom protocol for WebKit
✓ Same API surface as CDP/Juggler
✓ Safari technology stack
✓ Cross-platform consistency
```

**BROWSER → CONTEXT → PAGE HIERARCHY**
---------------------------------------

```
Browser Instance
├── Browser Process (Main process)
│   ├── Window management
│   ├── Profile data
│   └── Browser settings
│
├── Context 1 (Isolated Session)
│   ├── Cookies (isolated)
│   ├── Local Storage (isolated)
│   ├── Session Storage (isolated)
│   ├── Cache (isolated)
│   ├── Permissions (isolated)
│   │
│   ├── Page 1.1 (Tab/Window)
│   │   └── Renderer Process 1
│   ├── Page 1.2 (Tab/Window)
│   │   └── Renderer Process 2
│   └── Page 1.3 (Tab/Window)
│       └── Renderer Process 3
│
├── Context 2 (Isolated Session)
│   ├── Cookies (different from Context 1)
│   ├── Storage (different from Context 1)
│   │
│   └── Page 2.1 (Tab/Window)
│       └── Renderer Process 4
│
└── Context 3 (Isolated Session)
    └── Page 3.1 (Tab/Window)
        └── Renderer Process 5
```

**Hierarchy Explained:**

**Browser (Heavy Resource):**
- Actual browser application
- Takes seconds to launch
- Expensive to create
- One per worker process
- Shared across multiple tests

**Context (Lightweight):**
- Isolated browsing session
- Takes milliseconds to create
- Cheap to create
- One per test (automatic)
- Complete isolation from other contexts

**Page (Single Tab):**
- Individual web page/tab
- One page provided automatically
- Can create additional: context.newPage()
- Where you interact with DOM

**DATA FLOW - HOW ACTIONS WORK**
---------------------------------

**Example: `await page.click('#button')`**

```
Step 1: Your Code
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│ await page.click('#button')        │
└───────────┬────────────────────────┘
            │
Step 2: Playwright Library Processing
            ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. Parse selector: '#button'       │
│ 2. Query DOM for element           │
│ 3. Wait for element to exist       │
│ 4. Wait for element to be visible  │
│ 5. Wait for element to be enabled  │
│ 6. Scroll element into view        │
│ 7. Check element is actionable     │
│ 8. Check element is stable         │
└───────────┬────────────────────────┘
            │
Step 3: Protocol Commands (CDP/Juggler/WebKit)
            ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DOM.getDocument                    │
│ DOM.querySelector('#button')       │
│ DOM.getBoxModel (check visibility) │
│ Input.dispatchMouseEvent(click)    │
└───────────┬────────────────────────┘
            │
Step 4: Browser Execution
            ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Browser finds element in DOM       │
│ Browser synthesizes mouse events   │
│ Browser triggers JavaScript events │
│ Browser updates DOM/state          │
└───────────┬────────────────────────┘
            │
Step 5: Response Back
            ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Protocol: {success: true}          │
│ Playwright: Resolves Promise       │
│ Test: Continues to next line       │
└────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**AUTO-WAITING ARCHITECTURE**
------------------------------

**How Playwright Implements Auto-Waiting:**

```typescript
// When you call: await page.click('#button')

Internal Process:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Loop until timeout (30s default):       │
│                                         │
│ 1. Find element with selector           │
│    ├── If not found → Wait → Retry      │
│    └── If found → Continue               │
│                                         │
│ 2. Check if element is attached         │
│    ├── If detached → Wait → Retry       │
│    └── If attached → Continue            │
│                                         │
│ 3. Check if element is visible          │
│    ├── If hidden → Wait → Retry         │
│    └── If visible → Continue             │
│                                         │
│ 4. Check if element is stable           │
│    ├── If animating → Wait → Retry      │
│    └── If stable → Continue              │
│                                         │
│ 5. Check if element is enabled          │
│    ├── If disabled → Wait → Retry       │
│    └── If enabled → Continue             │
│                                         │
│ 6. Check if element is actionable       │
│    ├── If covered → Wait → Retry        │
│    └── If actionable → PERFORM ACTION   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘

All checks happen at browser level via protocols!
```

**PARALLEL EXECUTION ARCHITECTURE**
------------------------------------

```
Main Process (Test Runner)
│
├── Discovers all test files
├── Splits tests into groups
├── Spawns worker processes
│
├── Worker 1 (OS Process)
│   ├── Browser Instance 1 (Chromium)
│   │   ├── Context A → Test 1
│   │   ├── Context B → Test 2
│   │   └── Context C → Test 3
│   │
├── Worker 2 (OS Process)
│   ├── Browser Instance 2 (Chromium)
│   │   ├── Context A → Test 4
│   │   ├── Context B → Test 5
│   │   └── Context C → Test 6
│   │
└── Worker 3 (OS Process)
    ├── Browser Instance 3 (Chromium)
        ├── Context A → Test 7
        └── Context B → Test 8

All workers run in parallel!
Each test gets isolated context!
```

**Configuration:**
```typescript
// playwright.config.ts
export default defineConfig({
  workers: 4,  // Or '50%' for 50% of CPU cores
});
```

**NETWORK INTERCEPTION ARCHITECTURE**
--------------------------------------

```
Your Test Code
    │
    ├── page.route('**/api/*', route => {
    │     route.fulfill({ status: 200, body: '{}' });
    │   })
    │
    ▼
Playwright Library
    │
    ├── Registers route handler
    ├── Intercepts at protocol level
    │
    ▼
Browser Protocol
    │
    ├── Network.setRequestInterception(true)
    ├── Network.requestWillBeSent (event)
    ├── Network.continueRequest / fulfillRequest
    │
    ▼
Browser
    │
    ├── Request intercepted before network call
    ├── Playwright handler invoked
    ├── Mock response returned OR
    ├── Request fulfilled/aborted
```

**Benefits:**
- Intercept at browser level (no proxy needed)
- Fast (in-process)
- Reliable (no race conditions)
- Works with all browsers

**WHY PLAYWRIGHT IS FASTER THAN SELENIUM**
-------------------------------------------

**Selenium Architecture:**
```
Test Code (Node.js)
    ↓ HTTP Request (slow)
WebDriver Server (Java)
    ↓ JSON Wire Protocol (slow)
Browser Driver (chromedriver, geckodriver)
    ↓ Browser-specific protocol
Browser
```

**Playwright Architecture:**
```
Test Code (Node.js)
    ↓ Direct WebSocket (fast)
Browser Protocol (CDP/Juggler/WebKit)
    ↓ Binary Protocol (fast)
Browser
```

**Speed Comparison:**
- Playwright: 2-3x faster than Selenium
- No middleware (direct connection)
- Binary protocol (smaller payload)
- WebSocket (persistent connection)
- No HTTP overhead

**FILE SYSTEM ORGANIZATION**
-----------------------------

```
Your Project Root
│
├── node_modules/
│   ├── playwright/
│   │   ├── lib/                    ← Core library code
│   │   ├── types/                  ← TypeScript definitions
│   │   ├── .local-browsers/        ← Downloaded browsers
│   │   │   ├── chromium-1095492/
│   │   │   │   ├── chrome-linux/
│   │   │   │   └── INSTALLATION_COMPLETE
│   │   │   ├── firefox-1392/
│   │   │   │   ├── firefox/
│   │   │   │   └── INSTALLATION_COMPLETE
│   │   │   └── webkit-1877/
│   │   │       ├── webkit-linux/
│   │   │       └── INSTALLATION_COMPLETE
│   │   │
│   │   └── cli.js                  ← Playwright CLI
│   │
│   └── @playwright/test/           ← Test runner
│       ├── lib/                    ← Test runner code
│       ├── reporter/               ← Built-in reporters
│       └── index.js
│
├── tests/                          ← Your test files
│   ├── example.spec.ts
│   └── login.spec.ts
│
├── test-results/                   ← Test artifacts
│   ├── test-name/
│   │   ├── trace.zip              ← Trace files
│   │   ├── video.webm             ← Recorded videos
│   │   └── screenshot.png         ← Screenshots
│
├── playwright-report/              ← HTML report
├── playwright.config.ts            ← Configuration
└── package.json                    ← Dependencies
```

**ARCHITECTURE BENEFITS**
--------------------------

**1. Speed:**
- Direct browser protocols (no HTTP overhead)
- Binary communication (smaller payloads)
- Persistent WebSocket connections
- No middleware layers

**2. Reliability:**
- Auto-waiting built into protocol layer
- Actionability checks at browser level
- Automatic retries for transient failures
- No explicit waits needed

**3. Cross-Browser Consistency:**
- Single unified API
- Same behavior across browsers
- Playwright controls protocol implementation
- Guarantees consistency

**4. Modern Web Support:**
- Shadow DOM piercing
- Web Components support
- Service Workers handling
- Modern JavaScript features

**5. Developer Experience:**
- TypeScript support built-in
- Excellent debugging tools (Inspector, Trace Viewer)
- Detailed error messages
- Rich API documentation

**ARCHITECTURE SUMMARY**
------------------------

✅ **Three Layers:**
   - Test Code (Node.js)
   - Playwright Library (Automation Engine)
   - Browser (Chromium/Firefox/WebKit)

✅ **Communication:**
   - WebSocket-based protocols
   - Direct browser control
   - No HTTP/REST overhead

✅ **Hierarchy:**
   - Browser (Heavy, seconds) → One per worker
   - Context (Light, milliseconds) → One per test
   - Page (Single tab) → One or more per context

✅ **Isolation:**
   - Fresh context per test
   - Complete cookie/storage isolation
   - Safe parallel execution

✅ **Protocols:**
   - Chromium: Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)
   - Firefox: Juggler (Custom, by Playwright)
   - WebKit: WebKit Protocol (Custom, by Playwright)

✅ **Auto-Waiting:**
   - Built into protocol layer
   - 8 actionability checks
   - Automatic retries
   - No flaky tests

✅ **Parallel Execution:**
   - Workers (OS processes)
   - One browser per worker
   - Multiple contexts per browser
   - Tests run in parallel safely

**Key Takeaway:**
Playwright's architecture is designed from the ground up for modern web testing,
with direct browser control, automatic waiting, and complete test isolation,
making it faster and more reliable than traditional tools.
